ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 1.4.4.c SERVICE USES, TABLE 
5-1 ZONING DISTRICT USES, SECTION 5.7.4 CHILD CARE USES, AND 
TABLE 6-2 REQUIRED PARKING OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT 
CODE, CHANGING CHILD CARE REQUIREMENTS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on May 20, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 1.4.4.c Service Uses, of Article 1, General Provisions, of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
Family Child Care Home (FCCH). A Service use where preschool care and education is 
provided for up to 10 children within the owner’s primary residence. The owner’s infants, 
toddler and/or children ages three (3) to five (5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 
Family Child Care Center (FCCC). A Service use where preschool care and education is 
provided for up to 15 children within the owner’s primary residence. The owner’s infants, 
toddler and/or children ages three (3) to five (5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 
Child Care Center (CCC). A Service use where preschool care and education is provided 
for children on a regular basis not located in any person’s residence. 
 
Section 4.  That Section 5.1.4, Table 5-1 Zoning District Uses, of Article 5, Zoning 
Regulations, of the Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby 
amended as set out in this section: 
9:01 pm, May 20 2024

Page 2 of 4 
 
 
 

Page 3 of 4 
 
Section 5.  That Section 5.7.4 Child Care Uses, of Article 5, Zoning Regulations, of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
5.7.4 Child Care Uses 
In districts where child care uses are allowed, they shall meet the following standards in 
addition to all other lot and building standards for the district.  
 
a. Family Child Care Home (FCCH)  
1. Shall be located within the primary residence of the owner. 
2. No more than 10 children shall be permitted. 
3. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be 
met. 
 
b. Family Child Care Center (FCCC) 
1. Shall be located within the primary residence of the owner. 
2. No more than 15 children shall be permitted. 
3. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be 
met. 
4. Parking will be required per Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2. 
 
c. Child Care Center (CCC) 
1. Shall not be located within a residence. 
2. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be 
met. 
3. Parking will be required per Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2. 
 
d. Child Care provided as an accessory/secondary use to a commercial or industrial 
business is permitted in any zone district and must meet all requirements of the 
Wyoming Department of Family Services. 
 
Table 5-3: Reserved. 
 
Section 6.  That Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2, Required Automobile Parking, of Article 6, 
Design Regulations, Parking, Lot Access and Circulation, of the Unified Development Code of 
the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended to add Family Child Care Center (FCCC) and 
Child Care Center (CCC) with the following parking requirements: 
 
Family Child Care Center (FCCC) 
 
1 space per employee 
 
1 visitor space 
 
Child Care Center (CCC) 
 
1 space per employee 
 
1 space for each 20 children at maximum capacity 
 
Plus 2 required spaces

Section 7.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 8.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 1.4.4.c SERVICE USES, TABLE 
5-1 ZONING DISTRICT USES, SECTION 5.7.4 CHILD CARE USES, AND 
TABLE 6-2 REQUIRED PARKING OF THE UNIFIED DEVELOPMENT 
CODE, CHANGING CHILD CARE REQUIREMENTS." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne finds that the text amendment 
to the Unified Development Code (UDC) that is the subject of this ordinance meets the review 
criteria specified in Section 2.4.1.d of the UDC. 
 
Section 2.  That on May 20, 2024, the Planning Commission conducted a public hearing, 
reviewed the proposed text amendment, and recommended approval of said text amendment. 
 
Section 3.  That Section 1.4.4.c Service Uses, of Article 1, General Provisions, of the 
Unified Development Code of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby amended as set out in 
this section: 
 
 
Child Care, In-home – Minor Family Child Care Home (FCCH). A Service use where 
preschool care and education is provided for 3 up to 10 children for a portion of the day 
in a residential dwelling within the owner’s primary residence. The children are not 
related to the immediate family except that the provider’s own pre-school children shall 
count towards the total. The owner’s infants, toddler and/or children ages three (3) to five 
(5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 
Child Care, In-home – Major Family Child Care Center (FCCC). A Service use where 
preschool care and education is provided for 11 up to 15 children for a portion of the day 
in a residential dwelling within the owner’s primary residence. The children are not 
related to the immediate family except that the provider’s own pre-school children shall 
count towards the total. The owner’s infants, toddler and/or children ages three (3) to five 
(5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 
Child Care, Center – Minor. A Service use where preschool care and education is 
provided for under 30 children on a regular basis in a non-residential building. 
 
Child Care, Center – Major. A Service use where preschool care and education is 
provided for 30 children or more on a regular basis in a non-residential building. 
 
Child Care Center (CCC). A Service use where preschool care and education is provided 
for children on a regular basis not located in any person’s residence.

PLANNING COMMISSION  
MAY 20, 2024 
STAFF REPORT  
 
REQUEST: 
 
A Text Amendment amending the Unified Development Code Section 1.4.4.c, 5.1.4 
Table 5-1, 5.7.4 and 6.2.4 Table 6-2. 
 
CASE NUMBER:  
PLTA-24-5 
PREPARED BY: 
Connor White, Planner II, Planning and Development Department 
 
 
RECOMMENDED MOTION:  
 
Move to recommend the Governing Body approve text amendments to the Unified Development Code 
Sections 1.4.4.c, 5.1.4 Table 5-1, 5.7.4, and 6.2.4 Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and 
recommended by staff, noting the amendments meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.   
 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The City’s Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted by the Governing Body on January 23, 2012, by 
Ordinance Number 3943 with an effective date of April 30, 2012.  The Unified Development Code is the 
regulatory document carrying out the vision of PlanCheyenne and is reviewed annually pursuant to UDC 
1.1.7 [Annual Review Requirement] to determine if any particular standard or regulation is timely, 
relevant and adequately addresses current market conditions. In addition to the annual review, the 
Planning and Development Director and City Engineer are authorized under UDC 2.4.1.b.3 to bring 
forward text amendments on their own initiative.  
 
The proposed text amendments are to the Child Care requirements of the UDC. The proposed text 
amendments are to align UDC requirements with the Department of Family Services (DFS). The reason for 
the changes is due to the confusion as to what is required when someone wants to open up a new child 
care. The City’s regulations did not match what DFS required and made the process more difficult for new 
child cares.  
 
The first proposed changes are to the definitions. DFS has three types of Child Cares. Family Child Care 
Home, Family Child Care Center, and Child Care Center. Staffs proposed amendment is to match DFS with 
the definition of each. The UDC has four definitions for Child Cares which were similar to DFS but had 
different names and broke Child Care Centers into two, a minor and a major. Staff proposes changing 
Child Care, In-home-Minor to Family Child Care Home, change Child Care, In home-Major to Family Child 
Care Center, and change Child Care, Center – Minor and Major to just Child Care Center. DFS does not 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 
(Fax) 307-637-6366 

PLTA-24-5 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
delineate between a Child Care Center Minor or Major and just calls it a Child Care Center. These changes 
will make it less confusing as people read through DFS requirements and then look at the City zoning 
regulations as the names will be the same.  
 
The second recommended amendment is to allow Child Care Centers as Conditional Uses in the LR, MR, 
HR, NR-1, NR-2, and NR-3 zone districts. Staff’s reasoning is that the UDC allows Primary and Secondary 
schools as conditional uses within these zone districts and there only slight difference between a school 
and a child care. This would open up new opportunities for child cares in more locations while also having 
them go through a public process to determine if the location is suitable. Churches can often be found in 
residential zones, and a child care can be a weekday use of a church or a re-use opportunity of a church 
location should the church cease operation. 
 
The third recommended amendment is to 5.7.4, Child Care Uses. Staff proposes removing the table within 
this section and replacing it with standards that match DFS requirements. DFS has specific standards that 
all child cares are required to meet. Current UDC requirements add additional burdens to child cares that 
DFS does not require or allows alternatives to. For instance, the UDC requires an outdoor play area, where 
DFS allows for an increased indoor play area in place of not providing an outdoor play area. Staff feels that 
if an applicant is meeting all DFS requirements, the only child care specific zoning requirements we should 
really look at are the parking requirements (requirements standard to all uses in the zone district would 
still apply – such as applicable landscaping and pedestrian connections). The proposed amendments to 
Child Care Uses in 5.7.4 would also allow child care as a permitted accessory use to any permitted 
commercial or industrial use. This would allow a large-scale business to have a child care on-site for 
employees despite being in a zone which may not otherwise allow child care uses. 
 
The last recommended amendment is to add the parking requirements into UDC Section 6.2.4 Table 6-2. 
By placing the parking requirements into this section it would allow for additional ways to receive parking 
credits and flexibility. Staff proposes that Family Child Care Centers (in home) be required to have 1 space 
per employee and 1 visitor space. Staff proposes that Child Care Centers be required to have 1 space per 
employee, 1 space for each 20 children at maximum capacity, and 2 required spaces. As an example, if a 
Child Care Center has 60 children and 10 employees, the current UDC requirement would be 20 parking 
spaces. The ITE analysis would suggest only 14 parking spaces are needed. Staff’s proposed changes would 
require 15 parking spaces. A parking worksheet can be accessed here.   
 
PROPOSED AMENDMENTS: 
Amend UDC Section 1.4.4.c, Service Uses, specifically Child Care In Home – Minor and Major, and Child 
Care Center Minor and Major. 
 
Child Care, In-home – Minor Family Child Care Home (FCCH). A Service use where preschool care and 
education is provided for 3 up to 10 children for a portion of the day in a residential dwelling within the 
owner’s primary residence. The children are not related to the immediate family except that the 
provider’s own pre-school children shall count towards the total. The owner’s infants, toddler and/or 
children ages three (3) to five (5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 
Child Care, In-home – Major Family Child Care Center (FCCC). A Service use where preschool care and 
education is provided for 11 up to 15 children for a portion of the day in a residential dwelling within the 
owner’s primary residence. The children are not related to the immediate family except that the 
provider’s own pre-school children shall count towards the total. The owner’s infants, toddler and/or 
children ages three (3) to five (5) shall count toward the capacity. 
 

PLTA-24-5 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
Child Care, Center – Minor. A Service use where preschool care and education is provided for under 30 
children on a regular basis in a non-residential building. 
 
Child Care, Center – Major. A Service use where preschool care and education is provided for 30 children 
or more on a regular basis in a non-residential building. 
 
Child Care Center (CCC). A Service use where preschool care and education is provided for children on a 
regular basis not located in any person’s residence. 
 
Amend UDC Section 5.1.4, Table 5-1, to match the definition changes and allow Child Care Centers as 
Conditional Uses in the LR, MR, HR, NR-1, NR-2, and NR-3 zone districts. See attachment 1. 
 
Amend UDC Section 5.7.4, Child Care Uses to the following: 
 
In districts where child care uses are allowed, they shall meet the following standards in addition to all 
other lot and building standards for the district.  
(Delete existing table and replace with the following below.) 
 
a. Family Child Care Home (FCCH)  
1. Shall be located within the primary residence of the owner. 
2. No more than 10 children shall be permitted. 
3. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be met. 
 
b. Family Child Care Center (FCCC) 
1. Shall be located within the primary residence of the owner. 
2. No more than 15 children shall be permitted. 
3. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be met. 
4. Parking will be required per Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2. 
 
c. Child Care Center (CCC) 
1. Shall not be located within a residence. 
2. All requirements of the Wyoming Department of Family Services shall be met. 
3. Parking will be required per Section 6.2.4, Table 6-2. 
 
d. Child Care provided as an accessory/secondary use to a commercial or industrial business is 
permitted in any zone district and must meet all requirements of the Wyoming Department of 
Family Services. 
 
Amend UDC Section 6.2.4 Table 6-2, to add Child Care Parking requirements. See attachment 2. 
 
REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Review of a UDC text amendment shall be measured by the following three (3) criteria of Section 2.4.1.d: 
 
1. COMPREHENSIVE PLAN:  
“The amendment is in accordance with general goals and policies of the Comprehensive Plan, or 
is necessary to address an emerging issue not anticipated in the Comprehensive Plan and is not 
inconsistent with the plan.” 
 

PLTA-24-5 
 
 
May 20, 2024 
This proposed text amendment is necessary to address an issue not anticipated in the 
Comprehensive Plan when the Unified Development Code (UDC) was adopted. DFS has changed 
its requirements over the years and the UDC has not been updated to match what changes have 
been made. Plan Cheyenne encourages changes that align with market demand and child cares 
are in demand, but the current regulations can make them challenging to fit into locations 
throughout the City. 
  
2. STATE STATUTORY REQUIREMENTS:  
The amendment is consistent with the Wyoming Statutes, and specifically the Municipal Planning 
and Zoning statutes. The following Articles are all listed verbatim from Wyoming State Statute. 
 
WYO 15-1-601. 
The governing body of any city or town, by ordinance, may: 
 
(d) All regulations shall be made: 
(i) In accordance with a comprehensive plan and designed to: 
(A) Lessen congestion in the streets; 
(B) Secure safety from fire, panic and other dangers; 
(C) Promote health and general welfare; 
(D) Provide adequate light and air; 
(E) Prevent the overcrowding of land; 
(F) Avoid undue concentration of population; and 
(G) Facilitate adequate provisions for transportation, 
water, sewerage, schools, parks and other public 
requirements. 
 
Assessment: This amendment proposes modifying the Unified Development Code to align with 
DFS regulations and make things clearer for those looking to operate a child care.  
 
3. HEALTH, SAFETY, AND PUBLIC WELFARE:  
“The amendment promotes the general health, safety and public welfare.” 
 
This amendment provides aligning regulations with DFS and reducing parking requirements 
slightly to help promote more child cares throughout the community. These changes will make it 
easier for those looking to start up a child care as it would match DFS requirements, making the 
process less confusing.    
 
 
CONCLUSION:  
 
Staff concludes that this text amendment meets the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d. 
 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Staff recommends approval of text amendments to the Unified Development Code Sections 1.4.4.c, 5.1.4 
Table 5-1, 5.7.4, and 6.2.4 Table 6-2 as outlined in this staff report and recommended by staff, noting the 
amendments meet the review criteria of UDC 2.4.1.d.

Use
Parking Spaces
Assembly (auditorium, stadium, church, etc.)
1 space per each 5 seats (maximum capacity)
Bed and breakfasts, boardinghouse and/or lodging house
1 space per each 2 bedrooms
Hospitals
2 spaces per each bed
Hotels and motels
1 space for each rental unit plus 1 space for each 2 employees at maximum employment on a 
single shift
Plus 1 space for each company vehicle regularly parked on the premises
Accessory uses (dining, banquette) shall satisfy their parking requirement separately 
according to this table.
Industrial and Warehousing
1 space for each two employees at maximum employment on a single shift 
Plus 1 space for each company vehicle regularly parked on the premises
Nursing homes and assisted living facilities
1 space for each 5 beds for shared living facilities
1 space for each 3 independent living units
Plus 1 space for each employee at maximum employment on a single shift
Plus 1 visitor space for every 10 beds or 5 independent living units

< 50,000 square feet:  1 space per 300 square feet
≥ 50,000 square feet: 1 space per 400 square feet
Medical offices
1 space per 200 square feet

2 spaces per dwelling unit

1.5 spaces per dwelling unit

2,001 - 5,000 square feet: 10 spaces per 1,000 square feet
> 5,000 square feet: 20 spaces per 1,000 square feet
Plus 1 space per each 2 employees at maximum employment on a single shift.
Schools
K through 9:  1 space for each 15 students at maximum capacity
10 through 12:  1 space for each 4 students at maximum capacity
College and technical schools:  1 space for each 2 students at maximum capacity
Family Child Care Center (FCCC)
1 space per employee
1 visitor space
Child Care Center (CCC)
1 space per employee
1 spaces for each 20 children at maximum capacity
Plus 2 required spaces.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_54680186.pdf
ORDINANCE NO. ________ 
 
ENTITLED: "AN ORDINANCE CREATING SECTION 10.24.190, CRITICAL TRAFFIC 
CONTROL AREAS; CREATION AND ADMINISTRATION; ENHANCED 
PENALTIES, OF THE MUNICIPAL CODE OF THE CITY OF 
CHEYENNE, WYOMING, AUTHORIZING THE DESIGNATION OF 
CRITICAL 
TRAFFIC 
CONTROL 
AREAS 
AND 
AUTHORIZING 
ENHANCED PENALTIES AS SPECIFIED." 
 
 
BE IT ORDAINED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, 
WYOMING: 
 
Section 1.  That Section 10.24.190, Critical Traffic Control Areas; Creation and 
Administration; Enhanced Penalties, of Chapter 10.24, Rules of the Road Generally, of Title 10, 
Vehicles and Traffic, of the City of Cheyenne, Wyoming, is hereby created to read: 
 
10.24.190 - Critical traffic control areas; creation and administration; enhanced penalties. 
 
A. There is created the critical traffic control program, which shall be empowered to designate 
one (1) or more critical traffic control areas in the city of Cheyenne as provided in this 
section. The program shall be administered by the program committee in accordance with 
this section, which committee shall be composed of the mayor or his or her designee, the 
city engineer or his or her designee, and the chief of police or his or her designee. The 
objective of the program shall be to identify areas in the city with unsafe or unlawful traffic 
speeds or other behaviors and to implement appropriate temporary remedial measures to 
address those behaviors. 
 
B. No critical traffic control area shall be designated under the program except as provided in 
this subsection and only after the receipt of a critical traffic control program complaint. 
The complaint shall be signed by not less than five (5) residents of the area that is the 
subject of the complaint, which residents shall represent different businesses or households 
affected by the alleged unsafe or unlawful traffic speeds or behaviors. Complaint forms 
shall be made available to the public by the city engineer. Any complaint submitted under 
this subsection shall specifically identify the unsafe or unlawful traffic speeds or behaviors 
that are of concern to the residents. 
 
C. The program committee shall review and evaluate the merits of any traffic control program 
complaint submitted in accordance with subsection (B) of this section. Should the 
committee determine the complaint has merit, the city engineer shall order a traffic study 
for the affected area, the results of which shall be submitted to the committee not less than 
fourteen (14) days after the date that the study was initiated. If the city engineer ordered a 
study to evaluate vehicle speeds, the study period shall include not less than two (2) 
weekdays and not less than one (1) weekend Saturday as data gathering points. In addition, 
a crash history for not less than the previous five (5) years shall be obtained and a crash 
data analysis shall be developed and summarized. If the results of the study substantiate 
the complaint, the program committee may designate the affected area as a critical traffic 
3:51 pm, May 22, 2024

Page 2 of 3 
 
control area. Nothing in this subsection shall be construed to prohibit the city engineer from 
commissioning a third-party professional engineer to complete the traffic study required 
under this subsection. 
 
D. Upon the designation of any critical traffic control area as provided by subsection (C) of 
this section, the program committee may implement appropriate temporary remedial 
measures to address the unlawful traffic speeds or other behaviors including: 
 
1. Establishing enhanced fines for speeding violations within the designated area, which 
fines shall be two (2) times the amount of the fine that would be imposed for the same 
violation in the same or similar area but for the traffic control area designation. If 
enhanced fines are implemented, proper signage shall also be installed to provide 
reasonable notice to drivers, which signage shall identify the beginning and end points 
of the critical traffic control area. An enhanced fine imposed under this paragraph is 
mandatory and may only be reduced by a municipal court judge after the court 
specifically finds that the defendant has no ability to pay the full fine amount and that 
no reasonable probability exists that the defendant will have an ability to pay the full 
fine amount; 
 
2. Installation of additional traffic control devices, including driver feedback radar signs, 
traffic control signals or traffic calming devices; 
 
3. Increased law enforcement presence for the purpose of strictly enforcing traffic laws, 
which increased presence may be incentivized through the authorization of overtime to 
be offered to police officers; and 
 
4. Any other appropriate temporary measures the committee determines may address the 
unsafe or unlawful traffic behaviors. 
 
E. Any critical traffic control area designated under this section shall be reevaluated by the 
program committee not more than fourteen (14) days after the implementation of any 
temporary remedial measures to determine if the measures are still appropriate under the 
circumstances. Should the committee determine the unsafe or unlawful traffic behaviors 
have been corrected, the critical traffic control area designation shall terminate and the 
measures implemented in accordance with this section shall be removed. Should the 
committee determine that the temporary remedial measures should be modified or 
continued, the measures shall again be reevaluated by the program committee not more 
than fourteen (14) days after the measures were modified or continued. Any remedial 
measure implemented under this section may be instituted on a permanent basis when 
necessary to maintain the safety of the previously designated critical traffic control area 
and if made permanent, shall not be subject to the reevaluation requirements imposed by 
this subsection. 
 
F. Nothing in this section shall be construed to limit or otherwise affect the authority granted 
under title 10, chapter 10.68 of the city code or the authority of the city traffic engineer 
under section 10.12.130 of the city code.  

Page 3 of 3 
 
 
 
Section 2.  Sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases of this ordinance are 
severable. If any section, paragraph, sentence, clause, or phrase of this ordinance is declared 
unconstitutional or otherwise unlawful, such declaration shall not affect any of the remaining 
sections, paragraphs, sentences, clauses, and phrases. 
 
 
Section 3.  This ordinance shall be in full force and effect upon approval and publication.

THIRD AND FINAL READING: ____________________________________

PATRICK COLLINS, MAYOR

ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION ADOPTING THE FEE SCHEDULE SETTING THE 
APPROVED TOWING SERVICE RATES FOR TOWING CHARGES 
RELATED TO CALLS FOR SERVICE BY THE CHEYENNE POLICE 
DEPARTMENT MADE PURSUANT TO THE TOW ROTATION LIST." 
 
WHEREAS, Chapter 5.78, Towing and Wrecker Recovery Operations, of Title 5, Business 
Licenses and Regulations, of the Cheyenne city code regulates businesses providing motor vehicle 
towing and wrecker recovery operations offered to the public within the city;  
 
WHEREAS, Section 5.78.040 of the city code provides for the administration of the tow 
rotation list by the police department, which list identifies those approved towing and wrecker 
recovery operations that, on a rotating basis, will be requested by the department to respond to the 
scenes of accidents, incidents or emergencies involving motor vehicles for provided necessary 
towing and wrecker recovery services; 
 
WHEREAS, Section 5.78.040(F) of the city code requires the adoption a fee schedule by 
the Governing Body, setting the authorized service rates to be charged by towing and wrecker 
recovery operations for services provided in response to service calls made under the tow rotation 
list; 
 
WHEREAS, upon consultation with many of the towing and wrecker recovery operations 
operating in Cheyenne, the police department has proposed an updated fee schedule governing 
service rates under the tow rotation list, which schedule is attached hereto as Exhibit A; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body wishes to adopt the updated fee schedule attached hereto 
as Exhibit A as the fee schedule for towing charges related to calls for service made pursuant to 
the tow rotation list, specified under Section 5.78.040 of the city code; and 
 
WHEREAS, by adopting the attached fee schedule, it is the Governing Body's intent to 
repeal any and all prior resolutions setting a fee schedule for the tow rotation list, including 
Resolution No. 5548, adopted by the Governing Body on December 9, 2013. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the fee schedule attached hereto as Exhibit A is adopted, 
effective as of May 28, 2024, as the fee schedule for towing charges related to calls for service 
made pursuant to the tow rotation list under Section 5.78.040 of the city code; and 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, all prior resolutions setting a fee schedule for the tow 
rotation list, including Resolution No. 5548, adopted by the Governing Body on December 9, 2013, 
are hereby repealed and superseded by the present Resolution.

EXHIBIT A 
CITY OF CHEYENNE 
- Tow Rotation List Fee Schedule -  
 
This schedule specifies the maximum towing fee that may be imposed for towing services within the 
City of Cheyenne provided by towing/recovery operations when services are requested by the 
Cheyenne Police Department using the tow rotation list. No other fees or charges shall be allowed 
except as authorized under Section 5.78.040(F) of the city code. This fee schedule applies to passenger 
cars and light trucks and does not include large commercial vehicles and motor homes. In addition, 
this fee schedule does not apply to abandoned vehicle towing, the fees for which are governed under 
W.S. 31-13-111(g)(ii). 
These maximum towing rates apply to towing/recovery operations participating on the City's towing 
rotation list under Section 5.78.040 of the city code. City code does not mandate that these maximum 
rates be charged, but towing/recovery operations shall not charge an amount in excess of the fees 
specified below when providing services in response to a police Department service request. 
Notwithstanding the maximum fees specified below, a towing/recovery operation may charge fees in 
excess of—or in addition to—this schedule in accordance with Section 5.78.040(F) of the city code 
upon a showing of good cause and the: 1) Written approval of the police department prior to tow list 
participant providing the services giving rise to the excess charges; and 2) Submittal of sufficient 
documentation by the tow list participant as specified by the police department justifying the additional 
charges. The required documentation required under this paragraph shall be submitted to the police 
department not more than fifteen (15) calendar days after the date of the services provided by the 
towing/recovery operation.

Labor at scene.

additional truck needed:

and on holidays -

ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION EXPRESSING THE SUPPORT OF THE GOVERNING 
BODY FOR THE SUBMISSION OF AN APPLICATION BY THE 
CHEYENNE TRANSIT PROGRAM TO THE WYOMING DEPARTMENT 
OF TRANSPORTATION FOR GRANT FUNDING UNDER THE 
WYOMING RURAL PUBLIC TRANSIT PROGRAM." 
 
WHEREAS, in accordance with the Formula Grants for Rural Areas Program, codified at 
49 U.S.C. 5311 (the "Section 5311 program"), the Secretary of the federal Department of 
Transportation may make grants to assist states and local governmental authorities in financing 
capital, operating, planning, and job access and reverse commute projects, associated with 
providing public transportation in rural areas; 
 
WHEREAS, the Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT) administers the 
Section 5311 program for the State through its Rural Public Transit Program, which program 
provides state funding and federal formula grants to support development, maintenance and 
improvement of public transportation in rural and small urban areas; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne has previously received grant funding from WYDOT 
under the Rural Public Transit Program, most recently for Fiscal Year 2024 in the amount of three 
hundred thousand dollars ($300,000.00); 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne, acting through the Cheyenne Transit Program, wishes 
to again apply to WYDOT for financial assistance under the Rural Public Transit Program; and 
 
WHEREAS, if the grant application were approved, the Cheyenne Transit Program intends 
to use the grant funding to cover operational, administrative, and maintenance costs associated 
with providing public transportation in the City's service area in Laramie County. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED, that the Governing Body of the City of 
Cheyenne supports the Mayor executing and filing an application for financial assistance on behalf 
of the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Transit Program, with the Wyoming Department of 
Transportation to aid the financing of operating, project administration and maintenance assistance 
for projects under the Rural Public Transit Program; 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor is authorized to furnish such additional 
information as the Wyoming Department of Transportation may require in connection with the 
grant application; 
 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that the Mayor is authorized to execute any necessary 
grant and cooperative agreements with the Wyoming Department of Transportation on behalf of 
the City of Cheyenne and the Cheyenne Transit Program; AND

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_16394496.pdf
 
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Governing Body, City of Cheyenne 
FROM: 
 
Renee Smith, Economic Resource Administrator 
DATE: 
 
MAY 7, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
RESOLUTION SUPPORTING A WBC BRC GRANT APPLICATON 
 
Summary: 
The City of Cheyenne is seeking support for the submission of a $5M Wyoming Business Council, Business 
Ready Community Grant, due June 1, for the development of Precision Industrial Park, located within the 
Cheyenne Business Park. The infrastructure improvements will deliver fifty-five acres of shovel ready, 
industrial development that can provide up to eight (8) lots for mid-size to large tenant occupancy within 12 
months. Cheyenne LEADS has committed $3.4M in cash match to support the project which will be used for 
necessary land improvements.  
A public hearing will be held during the City Council Meeting on Tuesday, May 28, 2024 at 6:00 pm. 
About Cheyenne LEADS & Cheyenne Business Parks: 
The mission of Cheyenne LEADS is to make Cheyenne and Laramie County the location of choice for new 
and expanding businesses. Industrial Business Parks with shovel ready lots provide an opportunity to meet the 
timelines of those trying to move or expand their business. They create jobs, improve the tax base, aid in 
business retention, promote business diversity, and spur economic growth and clustering of like businesses. 
About the Wyoming Business Council BRC Grant and Loan Program: 
The Business Ready Community (BRC) grant and loan program provides financing for publicly owned 
infrastructure that serves the needs of businesses and promotes economic development within Wyoming 
communities. Public infrastructure eligible for funding includes water; sewer; roads; airports; rights of way; 
telecommunications; land; spec buildings; amenities within a business park, industrial park, industrial site or 
business district; landscaping, recreation, and educational facilities; and other physical projects in support of 
primary economic and educational development. 
If you would like additional information about the project, please contact me directly at 
rsmith@cheyennecity.org or (307)637-6285. 

Page 1 of 2 
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A 
RESOLUTION 
SUPPORTING 
THE 
SUBMISSION 
OF 
AN 
APPLICATION TO THE WYOMING BUSINESS COUNCIL UNDER THE 
WYOMING BUSINESS READY COMMUNITY GRANT AND LOAN 
PROGRAM FOR AN ENHANCEMENT GRANT IN SUPPORT OF THE 
DEVELOPMENT OF THE PRECISION INDUSTRIAL PARK." 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Wyoming Business Council administers the Business Ready Community 
grant and loan program (BRC Program), which program provides financing for publicly owned 
infrastructure that serves the needs of businesses and promotes economic development within 
Wyoming communities; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne desires to participate in the BRC 
Program to assist in financing the Precision Industrial Park project (the "Project"), which is to be 
situated within the Cheyenne Business Parkway located immediately east of central Cheyenne; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne recognizes the need for 
site-ready, industrial development that can provide for mid-size to large tenant occupancy in less 
than twelve (12) months and believes the Project will help address this need; 
 
WHEREAS, the specific goals and measures of success of the Project are the creation of 
fifty-five (55) acres of shovel ready land, including necessary improvements, for industrial 
purposes; 
 
WHEREAS, the public benefits of the Project will be the enhancement of workforce 
opportunities in the community and expanded tax revenues from businesses that choose Cheyenne 
for their relocations and expansion projects; 
 
 
WHEREAS, the BRC Program requires that certain criteria be met, as described in the 
Wyoming Business Council’s Rules governing the program, and based on information and belief 
the BRC Program application prepared by City staff meets those criteria; 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne is working in partnership with the Cheyenne-Laramie 
County Corporation for Economic Development, more commonly known as Cheynne LEADS. 
Cheyenne LEADS is a private, not-for-profit corporation serving as the economic development 
entity for the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County, Wyoming; 
 
WHEREAS, in partnership with the City, Cheyenne LEADS will provide three million 
four hundred thousand dollars ($3,400,000.00) in cash match in support of the Project, which 
funding will be used for land improvements; 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne has a comprehensive operation 
and maintenance plan for City owned infrastructure, including having procedures in place to 
account for projected expenses and projected income sources extending the life of the City assets; 
 
3:30 pm, May 09, 2024

Page 2 of 2 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body for the City of Cheyenne will use the revenue recaptured 
from the Project for the public benefit, including for economic development purposes; 
 
WHEREAS, in the event of any cost overruns attributable to the Project, the City of 
Cheyenne will provide funding in the amount necessary to complete the Project utilizing 
designated City funds; and 
 
WHEREAS, the City of Cheyenne held a public hearing on May 27, 2024, to solicit public 
comment from citizens and other stakeholders, and gave full consideration to all comments 
received. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City of Cheyenne supports the BRC Program grant 
application in the amount of five million dollars ($5,000,000.00) be submitted to the Wyoming 
Business Council for consideration of assistance in funding the Precision Industrial Park.  
 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Cheyenne Business Parkway, 13th Filing Expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the 
Final Plat to the Governing Body. 
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval

x

PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of a preliminary plat for roughly 69.33 acres of land. The subject 
property is currently vacant. The proposed plat would subdivide three parcels into 15 Lots, 14 of the Lots 
would be for Industrial use and 1 Lot for stormwater.  
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d  – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
On the future land use map found in PlanCheyenne, the subject land and surrounding area has 
been designated as Industrial. The Industrial designation encompasses existing and planned 
areas that include a range of employment-focused uses, which may have impacts such as noise, 
outdoor storage, and freight needs that make them incompatible in other areas of the 
community. Integration with the intermodal transportation network—rail, freight, and air—is an 
important factor in siting Industrial areas. 
 
Foundation 1 of PlanCheyenne encourages community growth. This development is in response 
to market demands and is within the Urban Service Boundary. The development patterns 
proposed by this plat are compatible with the designations found on the future land use map.

PUDC-24-52 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
 
Foundation 7 of PlanCheyenne supports the use of existing infrastructure and timely provision 
of services (including emergency and education services). This development is proposed in an 
area which has largely existing infrastructure and limited expansion of infrastructure is 
necessary. 
  
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed development arrangement is similar in lot size to other lots in the area. The 
proposed development creates an internal access easement that will allow access to all the lots. 
This development breaks up three large lots into smaller lots that will make development of the 
land simpler and will use the land more efficiently.    
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. Per UDC 4.2.5.c, fees paid at the time of a prior plat may be credited 
toward those due. This land was originally platted in 1999, at which time park fees were 
$500 per acre. Assuming plat-level park fees were appropriately paid in 1999, $28,700 can 
be credited toward this payment.  
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$20,090 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$20,090 ($350 per acre) 
Credit for fees previously paid  
 
- $28,700 ($500 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees 
$11,480 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$1,100.00 per acre (pro-rated to the nearest one-tenth 
of an acre) 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
This plat is occurring within an area of the community which has been previously developed. 
There is limited opportunity to modify the block layout or street network. The proposed plat 
does provide an access easement the will provide internal access for all the lots.  
 
Open Space (4.4): 
Open space is not required as the parcel is zoned HI Heavy Industrial.  
 

PUDC-24-52 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat appears to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. Easements are provided for BOPU utilities and for other utilities.  
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any commercial building on 
any of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees are payable at the 
issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage report was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns with the report 
provided. 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic worksheet was submitted and reviewed. The Engineering Department has requested a 
study be done. The applicant is working on a traffic study. The Engineering Department is okay 
with the plat moving forward but commented that no additional development will be allowed 
for the site without the Traffic Impact Study. The traffic study can be addressed with the Site 
Plans that get submitted for the site.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 

PUDC-24-52 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
Lot 15 is proposed to be a storm water detention lot and will be maintained by the property 
owner. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed 
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on 
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10) 
 
Remainder lots are not proposed with this plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved 
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access, 
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the 
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1) 
 
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat 
submitted. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.2) 
 
No conditions or modifications have been placed on the preliminary plat. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
   

PUDC-24-52 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.3.d.3) 
 
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered 
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above). 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1. 
 
STAFF REVIEW: 
 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1. Minor clerical corrections. 
 
CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant.  
   
ALTERNATIVES: 
 
1. Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body. 
2. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the 
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.  
3. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the 
preliminary plat to the Governing Body. 
4. Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and 
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.  
5. Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission 
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion, 
findings for denial must be stated. 
6. Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved. 
 
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the expedited Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the Governing 
Body. 
 

PUDC-24-52 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
ATTACHMENTS: 
1. Zoning Map  
2. Land Use Plan Map 
3. Master Application Form 
4. Preliminary Plat 
5. Final Plat

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND THE CITY 
CLERK TO SIGN A FINAL PLAT FOR CENTENNIAL HERITAGE, 3RD 
FILING, CITY OF CHEYENNE, LARAMIE COUNTY, WYOMING 
(LOCATED EAST OF SAVANNAH DRIVE AND SOUTH AND NORTH OF 
RALEIGH DRIVE)." 
 
 
WHEREAS, Gateway Rentals, LLC and Sun Valley Estates, LLC has subdivided said land 
in accordance with the statutes in such cases made and provided; 
 
 
WHEREAS, Gateway Rentals, LLC and Sun Valley Estates, LLC the owner of the above-
described land, has caused a subdivision plat of said land to be made, acknowledged, and certified, 
particularly describing the lots, blocks, easements, and rights-of-way; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the above-described subdivision plat has been presented to the City of 
Cheyenne Planning Commission for consideration; the Planning Commission has recommended 
that said plat be approved by the Governing Body; and the plat has been duly executed by the 
Development Office. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the subdivision described as Centennial Heritage, 3rd Filing, 
Cheyenne, Wyoming, be and the same hereby is approved and confirmed as presented, and that 
the Mayor and the City Clerk be and are hereby authorized, empowered, and directed to execute 
said plat, when Community Facility Fees have been paid in full. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)

____________________________________

CITY OF CHEYENNE PLANNING COMMISSION
MAY 6, 2024 
STAFF REPORT
FILE:
PUDC-24-6: Centennial Heritage, 3rd Filing (Expedited Review)
REQUEST:
Replat all of Lots 1 thru 7, Block 1, Centennial Heritage, 2nd Filing and a 
portion of Lot 1, Block 3, Centennial Heritage, 1st Filing.
LOCATION:
Along Raleigh Drive east of Savannah Drive. 
APPLICANT: 
Casey Palma
OWNER:
Gateway Rentals, LLC and Sun Valley Estates, LLC
ZONING:
MR Medium Density Residential District
PURPOSE: 
A replat from a Multifamily Project to Single-Family AD + SD fee-simple 
lots. 
PREPARED BY:
Sophia Maes, Planner I 
Planning and Development Department
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001
(Phone) 307-637-6282
(Fax) 307-637-6366

PUDC-24-6 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
RECOMMENDED MOTION: 
 
Move to approve the Centennial Heritage, 3rd Filing Preliminary Plat, advancing the Final Plat to the 
Governing Body with staff recommended conditions and acknowledgements.   
 
APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
UDC 2.1.2 Preliminary Plat Approval 
x 
UDC 2.1.3 Final Plat Approval  
x 
UDC Article 4 Subdivisions 
x 
UDC Article 5 Zoning Regulations 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting approval of an expedited preliminary plat for about 2.05 acres of land. The subject 
property is currently vacant land and is currently platted for multifamily development. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
Should the Planning Commission wish to recommend approval of the Preliminary Plat, the Commission 
must make the following findings required in UDC 2.1.2.d – a detailed analysis is below: 
 
1) The application is in accordance with the development policies and physical patterns 
identified in the Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.2.d.1) 
 
This development is in conformance with the principles and future land uses identified in 
PlanCheyenne. The property is within the Urban Service Boundary and has access to all utilities. 
The future land use map within PlanCheyenne identifies this area as Urban Residential, which is 
encouraged to develop with a mix of residential uses and densities. The plat provides medium 
density lots for townhome development.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
 
Future Land Use Designation  Zoning 
Land Use 
Subject Site 
Urban Residential 
MR Medium Density 
Residential 
Vacant 
North 
Urban Residential 
MR  
Single-family Detached 
Residential 
South 
Urban Residential / 
Industrial 
MR, HI Heavy Industrial  
Vacant, Railroad, Industrial 
East 
Urban Residential 
MR  
Vacant, Single-family 
Detached Residential 
West 
Urban Residential, Open 
Space 
MR, P Public 
Railroad / Vacant 

PUDC-24-6 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
2) The application reflects sound planning and urban design principles with respect to existing 
and potential future development in the vicinity, including consideration of current and future 
streets, open spaces, blocks and lots, and public utilities or facilities. (UDC 2.1.2.d.2) 
 
The proposed plat divides eight lots into eighteen lots. The northern lot (currently Lot 1, except 
the North 12 feet, Block 3, Centennial Heritage) is proposed to be divided into three (3) AD1 
lots, each with 25-foot frontages. The southern lots (Centennial Heritage, 2nd Filing Lots 1 thru 5, 
Block 1), are proposed to be divided into fifteen (15) lots (a mix of AD and SD lots).  
 
Townhomes mixed with single-family and duplex development creates a mix of housing options 
within an area which is considered beneficial from an urban design perspective.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
   
3) The division of land meets the purposes, intent, and all applicable standards in Article 4. (UDC 
2.1.2.d.3) 
 
Community Facility Fees (4.2): 
For fees based on acreage, the area of the plat is pro-rated to the nearest tenth of an acre. 
Lands reserved as unbuildable (for right-of-way, detention, parks, remainder lots, etc), are 
exempt from fees. 
Land Acquisition Fee  
 
 
 
$735 ($350 per acre) 
Infrastructure Fee  
 
 
 
$735 ($350 per acre) 
Total Land Acquisition & Infrastructure Fees  
$1,470  
(2.1 total acres) 
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, the Land Acquisition & Infrastructure fee are payable at 
the time of the signing of the final plat. Previously paid fees may be credited toward this 
payment. Centennial Heritage, 2nd Filing is assumed to have paid all applicable plat fees at 
the time it was filed; however, since this plat adds previously exempt land, a portion of the 
required fees will still be charged with this platting action.  
 
Per UDC Section 4.2.5, Table 4-2, Enhancement Fees are payable at the issuance of a 
building permit for each structure. 
Residential Enhancement Fee 
$400 per dwelling unit 
Commercial Enhancement Fee $1,100 per acre 
 
 
 
Transportation Network (4.3): 
The applicant does not propose any extensions or new connections. Townhome 
(“attached”) lots are not allowed front access if the lot is less than 36’ wide. The plat does not 
provide an access easement or alley for access to the central lots. These lots would need relief 
from UDC 4.3.6.c to allow front access. Block 1 is too large; however, Staff recommends the 
Planning Commission or City Council approve an exception due to the railroad proving a 
low potential for an effective connection through this property (per UDC 4.3.3.c.2). 
 
The applicant will need to provide an alley for rear-loading townhomes or provide a 
subdivision standards waiver. The plat proposes front access for townhome (AD1) lots, 
which is prohibited by UDC 4.3.6.c. The applicant has submitted a subdivision standards 
waiver to request relief. 
 

PUDC-24-6 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
Open Space (4.4): 
With the Centennial Heritage, 2nd Filing plat, a parcel of land south of this proposed 
development was dedicated to the City Greenway and this proposed infill project will 
provide a connection to the Greenway trail.  
 
Required Improvements (4.5): 
This plat does appear to be able to provide all required utilities to the lots within this 
development. However, the preliminary plat shows water services set between sanitary 
services, which could present future undesired maintenance access issues (at the corner of 
Raleigh Drive and Taft Avenue). Water services will also need to be discussed for civil plans. 
These issues will be coordinated and resolved with construction. 
 
The submitted plat does not show an ‘escape’ easement for Black Hills Energy to make its 
system ‘whole’ as the development is built (for which an 8-foot-wide easement along the 
west property line of Lot 8, Block 1 was requested). The applicant and Black Hills Energy 
need to coordinate to resolve the issue and add any required easements to the plat.  
 
 
Public Safety Fees (4.6): 
Public Safety Fees are required at the time of construction of any commercial building or 
residential building on any of the proposed lots. Per UDC Section 4.6.3.b, Public Safety Fees 
are payable at the issuance of a building permit for each structure.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
 
4) Any phasing proposed in the application is clearly indicated and demonstrates a logical and 
well-planned development pattern. This may include consideration of amenities and public 
facilities of the subdivision within each phase compared to the anticipated timing or the 
likelihood of future phases being constructed as planned. (UDC 2.1.2.d.4) 
 
No specific phasing is proposed with this project. 
 
Review criterion not applicable.   
 
5) The impacts identified in specific studies or technical reports that were prepared or requested 
to be prepared in association with the application, and whether the findings and 
recommendations of these reports, or any modifications or conditions on approval of the 
application, can mitigate these impacts. (UDC 2.1.2.d.5) 
 
Drainage: 
A drainage study was submitted and reviewed. The applicant has proposes a drainage easement 
for Lot 15, Block 1, which will have shared ownership between all fee-simple lot owners Lots 1 
thru 14, and will provide a blanket easement for open space, drainage, utilities, and public or 
private facilities.  
 
The applicant provided a letter from the engineer of record indicating the proposed changes to 
the plat are in conformance with the previously approved subdivision drainage plan. 
 
 
 

PUDC-24-6 
 
 
May 6, 2024 
 
Traffic: 
A traffic study was submitted and reviewed. There were no concerns about the study provided. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions. 
  
6) Lots proposed for development shall be buildable lots according to the existing or proposed 
zoning district standards, result in design and development patterns that meet the Intent and 
Applicability statements of the existing or proposed zoning district, and not aggravate 
nonconforming setbacks or currently adopted building and fire code access and separation 
requirements. (UDC 2.1.2.d.6) 
 
This plat proposes seventeen (17) buildable lots according to the MR Medium Residential Zone. 
Lots 1-14, Block 1 and Lots 1-3, Block 2 are proposed for residential development of townhomes 
and semi-attached duplexes, which are permitted as-of-right in the MR Zone.  
 
All lots proposed for development are buildable lots in this zone district. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
7) All parcels not proposed as buildable lots and which are not dedicated to and accepted by the 
City shall have a clearly defined entity responsible for ongoing maintenance.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.7) 
 
Lot 15, Block 1 is proposed to be a non-buildable lot. Note #5 on the plat indicates that it will 
serve as a blanket easement for open space, drainage, utilities, and public or private shared 
facilities. This lot will be established by equal, undivided interest of all owner(s) of fee-simple 
Lots 1 thru 14, Block 1.  
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
8) The application will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development for 
adjacent property considering the existing zoning and Comprehensive Plan policies for that 
property.  (UDC 2.1.2.d.8) 
 
This plat will not create any undue influence on existing or potential development. The lots are 
proposed for future single unit residential development which is similar to what is in the area as 
well.   
 
The application complies with this review criterion.  
 
9) The application is not harmful to the public health, safety, and welfare. (UDC 2.1.2.d.9) 
 
City staff is satisfied that this proposed plat would not adversely affect the public health, safety, 
or general welfare. 
 
The application complies with this review criterion.   
 
 
 

PUDC-24-6 
May 6, 2024 
10) Remainder lot(s) shall be clearly identified on the preliminary plat and shall not be developed
or proposed for development for purposes.  Remainder lot(s) shall be noted and described on
all plats either graphically or by legal description. (UDC 2.1.2.d.10)
This plat does not propose any remainder lots.
Review criterion not applicable.
11) The layout and design of the final plat is in substantial compliance with the approved
preliminary plat considering the number of lots or parcels, street and block layout, and access,
and any deviations in the final plat brings the application in further compliance with the
Comprehensive Plan. (UDC 2.1.3.d.1)
The Final Plat submitted with the application is substantially similar to the Preliminary Plat
submitted.
The application complies with this review criterion.
12) The application meets any conditions or modifications of the approved preliminary plat. (UDC
2.1.3.d.2)
Through the review process and application of the Preliminary Plat, Staff recommends one
condition to the plat. This correction should be resolved prior to final plat approval.
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.
13) The application meets the purposes, intent, and applicable standards of Article 4. (UDC
2.1.3.d.3)
As a concurrent Preliminary and Final Plat application, this Final Plat review criterion is covered
by the equivalent Preliminary Plat review criterion (subsection 3 above).
The application complies with this review criterion, with conditions.
CONFORMANCE WITH APPLICABLE PLANS: 
The project is located in an area covered by the following plans: 
x
PlanCheyenne
Conformance with PlanCheyenne is covered by review criterion 1.
STAFF REVIEW: 
Through the review process, comments were received regarding the following issues: 
1.
Delineation of an 'escape' easement of 8 feet in width along the west property line of Lot 8
Block 1 to allow Black Hills Energy to make system 'whole' again as the development is built.
2.
Plat shows water services set between sanitary services, which could present future undesired
maintenance access issues (at the corner of Raleigh Drive and Taft Avenue).
3.
Water services will need to be discussed for civil plans.
CONCLUSION: 

PUDC-24-6 
May 6, 2024 
Staff analysis is that the application meets the review criteria and therefore recommends approval of 
the expedited plat application as submitted by the applicant with conditions. 
As noted in Criterion 3, the applicant has requested relief via a subdivision standards waiver for front-
loading townhomes. Staff would ask that it be taken into consideration when reviewing the plat.   
Also in Criterion 3, the plat fails to show a delineation of an 8-foot 'escape' easement for Black Hills 
Energy along Lot 8 Block 1.  
ALTERNATIVES: 
1.
Recommend approval of the expedited preliminary plat, forwarding the Final Plat to the
Governing Body.
2.
Recommend approval of the preliminary plat with conditions and that a final plat addressing the
conditions of approval be prepared and presented to the Governing Body.
3.
Recommend approval of the preliminary plat as submitted by the applicant and forward the
preliminary plat to the Governing Body.
4.
Recommend approval of the preliminary plat subject to staff’s recommended conditions and
forward the preliminary plat to the Governing Body.
5.
Recommend denial of the preliminary plat based on findings of denial. The Planning Commission
must conclude that the project does not meet all the review criteria. As part of the motion,
findings for denial must be stated.
6.
Postpone the project until issues identified during the meeting can be resolved.
General Information Regarding Alternatives: 
The Planning Commission may modify staff’s recommended conditions, delete conditions, add 
conditions, or decide contrary to staff’s recommendation. In the event conditions are modified, the 
conditions shall relate to applicable review criteria. In the event that the Commission acts contrary to 
staff’s recommendation or modifies conditions of approval, the Commission shall cite specific reasons 
(based on the review criteria) for approval or denial. 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
Staff recommends approval with the following conditions: 
1.
The following exception to be granted: Exception to Block size
2.
Acknowledgement of relief: Access to a street for lots less than 36’ wide (Subdivision Standards
Waiver)
3.
Prior to City Council approval: Zesolve the issue with Black Hills Energy utilities and easements 
tothe satisfaction of Black Hills Energy.
ATTACHMENTS: 
1.
Zoning Map
2.
Land Use Plan Map
3.
Master Application Form
4.
Preliminary Plat
5.
Final Plat

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION APPROVING SANITARY SEWER SERVICE, BY 
OUTSIDE USER AGREEMENT, FOR LARAMIE COUNTY, FOR THE 
PROPERTY KNOWN AS THE ARCHER COMPLEX (SOUTH OF THE I-
80 AND ARCHER PARKWAY INTERCHANGE)." 
 
 
WHEREAS, Laramie County is the owner of the property in Laramie County, Wyoming, 
known as the Archer Complex, more specifically described as:

WHEREAS, Laramie County ("the Applicant") submitted an application to the City of 
Cheyenne and the Board of Public Utilities ("BOPU") for water and/or sanitary sewer services 
outside of the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne for the above-referenced properties; 
 
 
WHEREAS, pursuant to Resolution No. 3577, BOPU was instructed to present to the 
Governing Body for its review any application for the provision of water and/or sanitary sewer 
services to any individual or entity outside the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne; 
 
 
WHEREAS, BOPU has reviewed the application submitted by the Applicant, and BOPU 
and the Applicant will approve and execute applicable Outside User Agreement(s) upon approval 
by the Governing Body pursuant to Resolution No. 3577; and 
 
 
WHEREAS, the Governing Body has reviewed the applications for sanitary sewer services 
submitted by the Applicant and has determined that approval is in the best interest of the City of 
Cheyenne. 
 
 
NOW, THEREFORE BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, THAT the application submitted by the Applicant to BOPU for 
sanitary sewer services outside of the corporate limits of the City of Cheyenne for the property 
known as the Archer Complex and more specifically described above, is hereby approved. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED, that BOPU and the Applicant are authorized to approve 
and execute Outside User Agreement(s). 

4

APPLICABLE CITY CODE SECTION(S) AND PLANS: 
 
x 
PlanCheyenne 
x 
Resolution 3577 
 
BACKGROUND: 
 
The applicant is requesting connection to BOPU sanitary sewer services only for property outside City limits. 
This property is the Archer Complex, there is currently no existing sewer in the area or other outside user 
agreements. 
 
SURROUNDING LAND USE AND ZONING: 
 
 
The property is currently zoned a PUD Planned Unit Development in the County.  
 
ANALYSIS OF APPLICATION CONFORMANCE WITH REVIEW CRITERIA: 
 
The request for an outside user agreement has no formal review criteria; however, the following 
elements should be considered when reviewing outside user agreements: 
- 
BOPU capacity 
- 
Needed infrastructure improvements 
- 
Population served 
- 
Considerations on the effect on future annexation(s) 
- 
Fiscal cost/benefit to the City and the BOPU 
- 
Conformance with the Comprehensive Plan 
 
The requested outside user agreement has minimal impact on BOPU’s system capacity. The proposed 
use of the property includes light and medium density commercial and industrial facilities. The current 
existing facilities consist of a mix of administrative County owned facilities and the County Fair Grounds. 
The closest sewer connection is located at either Hutchins Drive or Venture Drive, which is on the north 
side of I-80 and to the West. This connection can be used or another connection can be proposed.

May 13, 2024 
Property tax would increase, but the City would not have any income from the property tax in the area. 
The BOPU may charge a higher rate for outside users. The sewer infrastructure will need to be extended 
to this area as it does not exist already, and Laramie County will be responsible for extending the 
infrastructure. City police, fire, and sanitation services are not within this area as it is solely within the 
County, so if this parcel were to come into City limits by itself, it would likely result in a need to extend 
the services to this area. 
 
The Comprehensive Plan designates this area as Mixed-use Employment – and the proposed use in this 
location is consistent with that anticipated development pattern.

CONCLUSION: 
 
Staff is satisfied that the application is acceptable based on the considerations specific to this request 
and therefore recommends approval of the outside user agreement.   
 
STAFF RECOMMENDATION: 
 
Staff recommends approval of the proposed outside user agreement.

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED:  "A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE BUDGET OF THE CHEYENNE 
REGIONAL AIRPORT BOARD FOR THE FISCAL YEAR BEGINNING 
JULY 1, 2024 AND ENDING JUNE 30, 2025." 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board ("Board") was created pursuant to 
Ordinance No. 2407 adopted by the Governing Body on February 11, 1980, as amended; 
 
WHEREAS, City Code Section 2.60.050(B) sets forth the requirement that the Board shall 
submit on or before the first day of May of each year, to the governing bodies of the city and the 
county, a proposed budget for the ensuing fiscal year; 
 
WHEREAS, City Code Section 2.60.050(B) provides that the Board budget shall be subject 
to alteration, modification, and approval by the Governing Body in accordance with the policies 
of the City; and 
 
WHEREAS, City staff has reviewed the proposed budget received from the Board. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the proposed budget for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 
2024 and ending June 30, 2025 of the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board, as set forth in the attached 
information with this Resolution entitled "Cheyenne Regional Airport Operating Budget for the 
Fiscal Year 2025" in the total amount of ten million eight hundred seven thousand fifty-five dollars 
and seventy-five cents ($10,807,055.75), is hereby approved. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
10:24 am, May 07 2024

 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Cheyenne Regional Airport 
Operating Budget FY 2025 
 

1 
 
Table of Contents 
BACKGROUND 
2 
CHEYENNE REGIONAL AIRPORT BOARD BUDGET SUMMARY 
3-6

9-10 
FIXED-BASED OPERATIONS BUDGET 
11-13 
CAPITAL BUDGET 
13 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 

2 
 
Background 
The following pages present the Cheyenne Regional Airport Board’s operating budget 
for fiscal year 2025. The Board consists of five members, two members appointed by 
the City of Cheyenne, two members appointed by Laramie County, and one member 
appointed by the City and County in alternating terms. The Joint Powers Agreement 
sets forth the terms by which the City, County, and Airport operate. Under this 
agreement, the City and County annually review the Airport Budget. The City and 
County also sign all FAA grant agreements as co-sponsors. The Board operates 
under the authority of both an Airport Board under Wyoming State Statute and as a 
Joint Powers Board and holds ownership of all facilities, equipment, lease holdings, 
and operating rights. 
 
The Board adopted a Certificate of Organization on July 1, 1980, pursuant to the City 
of Cheyenne Ordinance 2407 and Laramie County Commissioners Resolution 
officially forming the Airport Board and electing officers under W.S.§ 10-4-101 through 
10-4-107. Under W.S.§ 16-1-104(d), the Airport Board was deemed a joint powers 
board.  
 
For the year January 1, 2024 – December 31, 2024, the slate of officers is: Kevin 
Paintner, President; Dr. Jeff Storey, Vice President; Melissa Amick, Treasurer; Kim 
Brokaw, Secretary; and Keith Zabka, Member.  
 
The Board’s fiscal year is from July 1 – June 30 each year. Once the Board approves 
the budget it is submitted to the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County for review by 
May 1st of each year.  
 
The Board operates the Airport as a business enterprise to be financially self-
sufficient. The Airport does not have the authority to tax and does not use local tax 
dollars, property tax or sales tax. The Airport is funded through fees paid by airport 
users, including airlines and businesses that operate at the airport.  
 
Community/Economic Impacts 
The airport is a key piece of infrastructure for our community, connecting citizens and 
employers to the country and globally. The airport supports not just passenger travel, 
but also the Wyoming Air National Guard and the State of Wyoming, among many 
other consumers. According to the Wyoming 2020 Aviation Economic Impact Study, 
Cheyenne Regional Airport’s commercial air services and airport activities provide 
over $27.5 million in annual economic activity to the community, including $4.98 
million for business tenants.  Wyoming airports provide over 20,000 jobs.   
Additionally, Wyoming airports have approximately 3,628 business tenants across the 
state.  Included in the amount are the approximate 60 tenants at the Cheyenne 
Regional Airport including: 
 
• Land leases to 21 various tenants including the Wyoming Air National Guard, 
the Wyoming Department of Transportation, and the City of Cheyenne.   
• Hangar rentals to 12 tenants including Alpine Air, the Flying School, and Reach 

3 
 
Air Medical Services. 
• Commercial leases to 29 tenants including various doctors, dentists, the US 
Postal Service, DFS, and WYDOT Aeronautics. 
 
Cheyenne Regional Airport Board Budget Summary 
Impacts 
Air Service 
The past few years have had a significant impact on the Airport’s air service.  During 
2020, we saw traveler numbers reduced due to the COVID-19 pandemic.  From 2020-
2023 we saw decreased travelers due to the runway reconstruction and several 
required full and partial runway shutdowns.  
 
In September 2023, the Airport’s final phase of the runway reconstruction was 
completed, and the Airport was able to return to full air service.  Even with no air 
service from April to September, the Airport saw 12,992 emplacements.  In 2022, there 
were 19,511 enplanements. Prior to the pandemic and runway reconstruction, there 
were 15,888 enplanements in 2019. At this time there is no anticipated future impact to 
air service.   
 
Summary 
This year we are presenting the budget with two enterprise centers. The enterprise 
centers include Airport Administration and Operations and Fixed Based Operations 
(FBO). Revenues, and expenses for the various enterprise centers and their 
associated cost centers are outlined in the following pages.  
 
Presented on pages 4-6 is a chart summarizing the Airport Board’s total budget for the 
fiscal year 2024 (July 1, 2024-June 30, 2025). We are expected to have net operating 
income of $340,362 with a net loss of $656,319 including all capital expenses.   
 
The blue color indicates that it is an amount received/paid by the Airport.  The orange 
color indicates amounts received/paid using other funds such as 6th penny funds, 
Wyoming Air National Guard, federal, and state grants.
