Airport Administration and Operations  
The Airport’s operating budget is a stand-alone enterprise center separate from the 
FBO operations. All revenues generated from the operation of this enterprise center, 
including rents, commercial landing fees (other than FBO landing fees), and other 
operating fees, will be used to offset the expenses related to airport operations. This 
budget year the airport will use Airport reserves for operating/capital expenses in 
excess of revenues.  
 
The income (loss) from airport operations for FY 2025 is anticipated to be $268,491.   
The specific details for the airport administration and operations enterprise center are 
outlined on pages 7-10. The Airport’s capital plan can be found on page 13. 
Revenue 
Aeronautical revenues are collected from both the fixed base operator and the airlines 
as well as other aeronautical users of the airport. Non-aeronautical revenues are 
collected from a variety of sources including terminal concessions, rental cars, and 
lease revenue. These revenue streams are used to fund cost-center expenses. There 
are five revenue centers for airport administration and operations including air 
carriers, leased space, rental cars, concessions, and other income.  The total amount 
of revenue expected to be collected by Administration and Operations is $3,101,309.  A 
more detailed review of the anticipated revenues and sources for each of these profit 
centers is below. 
 
Air Carriers 
Air Carrier income is derived from three sources: landing fees, terminal rents, and 
Passenger Facility Charges (PFC). Landing fees and terminal rent revenues are used 
to offset expenses related to the airfield and terminal operations. PFC revenues are 
used to reimburse the Airport for approved projects previously completed.    
 
Due to the inconsistent air carrier income over the past several years, a conservative 
estimate is $170,664 for fiscal year 2025. Of this, $64,056 is terminal space rental, 
$25,044 is landing fees, and $81,564 are PFC’s.  
  
Leased Space 
The Airport currently has approximately 70 leases with various tenants including the 
Wyoming Department of Transportation (WYDOT), Wyoming Air National Guard 
(WANG), the City of Cheyenne, and the State of Wyoming. The leased space 
includes building rent, land rent, hangar rent (excluding community hangar), terminal 
rent (excluding rental cars and air carriers), and storage rent.   The leased space 
revenue is used to offset the expenses in the “Leased Space” cost center.   
 
The budget does include a 3% annual increase in rent for inflation but this may vary 
as we are unsure what the Consumer Price Index (CPI) will be during FY 2025.  
Leased space revenue for fiscal year 2025 is anticipated to be $2,364,337. 
 
Rental Cars 
Rental car revenue is predominantly composed of minimum annual guaranteed 
amounts (MAGs). The on-airport rental car companies are required to pay 10% of 

8 
 
gross revenue or their MAG, whichever is higher. This is included in the concession 
revenues below.   
 
The rental cars also pay rent for space in the terminal which is used to offset expenses 
in the “Terminal” cost center. The rental cars pay rent and utility costs for the use of 
the wash bay facility. These fees help to offset the cost of operating the wash bay 
which is included in the “Leased Space” cost center. The amount of rent revenue from 
rental cars for fiscal year 2025 is anticipated to be $8,226. 
 
The third type of rental car revenue is Customer Facility Charges (CFC) which are 
collected and held to offset rental car-related approved projects. This revenue is 
anticipated to be $94,976.   The rental car revenue for fiscal year 2025 is anticipated 
to be $103,202 excluding the concessions. 
 
Concessions 
Usually, most of the income from concessions is received from restaurant revenue. At 
this time, there is no active restaurant, so no restaurant concessions have been 
incorporated into this budget. Other concession revenue includes rental car 
concessions, ATM concessions, vending machine concessions, and ride-share 
concessions.   
 
All concession revenue is dependent on activity level and is hard to predict depending 
on market fluctuations. The concession revenue stream helps to fund the “Terminal” 
cost center. Revenue is budgeted to be $180,364 for fiscal year 2025. 
 
Other Income 
Other income includes interest income, advertising income, sixth penny sales tax (still 
being collected for parking lot) and other airport revenue such as badging fees. The 
budgeted amount for fiscal year 2025 is $282,742.

FY 25 ADMINISTRATION AND OPERATIONS REVENUE

9 
 
Expenses 
Budgeted expenses for airport administration and operations total $2,804,038. 
Operating expenses exclude FBO expenses and capital expenses as those are reflected 
in separate budgets on pages 11-13 following the detailed cost center information. 
 
The number of full-time equivalent staff for Airport administration and operations (non-
FBO) employees is currently 11 with two vacancies expected to be filled for this 
upcoming fiscal year 2025 and incorporated into the budget. Incorporated into the 
budget for both administration/operations and FBO is a 5% raise for all full-time 
employees which will ultimately vary from 2.5% to 5% depending on their annual 
employee evaluation.   Finally, there is a 5% adjustment, for both 
administration/operations and FBO, for employer-provided benefits excluding health 
insurance which is not anticipated to increase for FY 25 unless employees change 
from their current selection (i.e. individual or family) in the course of the year. 
 
Also incorporated into this budget is an increase in insurance premiums, for both 
administration/operations and FBO.  Per discussions with our insurance company, 
rates in the area are expected to increase 25-30% for FY 25.  We increased our 
budget for employee training and IT hardware to incorporate the purchase of new 
computers that are hitting their life cycle.  Finally, we increased the budget for 
repairs/maintenance and supplies as aging infrastructure requires additional repairs.   
 
There are five direct cost centers for the Airport: Administration, airfield, leased space, 
other buildings and grounds, and terminal. There are 16 corresponding expense 
categories. The expense categories include areas such as personnel expenses, 
utilities and building expenses, operations and maintenance expenses, and 
equipment expenses. 
 
Administration and Operations Cost Center 
This cost center includes all administrative and operations expenses that were not 
specifically assigned to another cost center.  This also includes expenses related to 
administrative and operations staff expenses.   
 
Airfield Cost Center 
This cost center includes the costs associated with the airfield and air carrier apron 
including snow removal, aircraft rescue firefighting, operations and maintenance 
expenses, and utilities expenses. This cost center is offset by revenues collected by 
Air Carriers.   
 
Leased Space Cost Center 
This cost center includes all expenses related to our leased property.  This cost 
center also includes the airport-owned hangars, the rental car wash bays as well as 
aeronautical and non-aeronautical tenants. This cost center is offset by revenues 
collected by the Rental Cars and Leased Space revenue centers.   
 
Terminal 
Included in the terminal cost center are all costs associated with the terminal building 

10 
 
including repair and maintenance, custodial, utilities, the parking lot, baggage system 
and certain security items related to access control (cameras, doors, alarms). This 
cost center is offset by revenues collected by Air Carriers, Rental Cars, and 
Concessions revenue centers.  
 
Other Buildings and Grounds 
This cost center includes the expenses associated other grounds at the airport 
including the parks and grassy areas as well as roadways at the Airport.

The Fixed Based Operations (FBO) operates as a separate enterprise center. It is

Revenues 
The FBO has the following revenue centers: Fuel sales (Skywest, Military, Contract, 
Other), FBO services, and miscellaneous revenue. 
 
Revenues for the FBO are hard to predict due to the past several years being 
inconsistent.  For a conservative estimate, we averaged FY22 and FY23 revenue for 
FBO Services and Misc. Revenue.   
 
Fuel revenues were estimated based on partial FY 23 and FY 22 sales due to our 
runway closure in FY 22, and factored in a 10% decrease due to trends across the 
nation for FBOs.   
 
Expenses 
The FBO has the same cost centers as the Administration and Operations enterprise 
center. The FBO has seven full-time employees and three part-time employees with 
an additional part-time employee anticipated to be hired in the next few months.  Fuel 
costs are based on a combination of FY 23 and FY 22 expenses and are decreased 
by 10% decrease due to trends across the nation for FBOs. Unfortunately, this is 
extremely hard to predict as we are unsure what fuel prices will be in the upcoming 
year.   
 

13 
 
 
Airport Capital FY 2025 Budget 
Capital expenses such as airfield equipment purchases and airfield construction costs 
are typically funded through the Capital Improvement Program (CIP). Capital funds 
include the Federal Airport Improvement Program (AIP), state grant funds, 6th penny 
funds, Passenger Facility Charges (PFCs), and rental car Customer Facility Charges 
(CFCs).  All the proposed projects for FY 2025 are detailed below.   
 
   
Cash Balance Forecast 
As of March 31, 2024, the Board has $2,883,244 in cash reserves.  Of that, $874,377 
is currently restricted in investments and unavailable until October 2025.  As indicated 
in the overall budget on page 6, approximately $656,319 will need to come from 
reserves or debt services. Currently, the Airport has no outstanding debt service. Of 
that amount, $86,222 is required to be paid for capital projects for our local match.  
 
If using available cash reserves, the projected remaining cash balance at the end of 
FY 2025 would be $1,352,548.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_57623349.pdf
Planning and Development Office 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282   
(Fax) 307-637-6366 
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
To: 
Honorable Mayor Patrick Collins and Members of the City Council 
From: 
Charles W. Bloom, AICP, Planning and Development Director  
Subject: Downtown Development Authority Proposed Budget - Fiscal Year 2025 
Date: 
May 13, 2025 
 
Background:  
 
Attached is the proposed budget for fiscal year 2025 (July 1 to June 30). The proposed Governing Body 
review schedule is as follows: 
• 
Introduction and Referral: May 13, 2025 
• 
Public Services Committee: May 20, 2025 
• 
Final consideration: May 28, 2025 
 
The budget represents a $76.649.16 increase in mill levy assessment collections ($463,649.16) and an 
increase in interest earned. The interest earned estimate is based on an estimated $1 million dollars 
being left in the Wyoming Government Investment Fund (WGIF) which as of April 12, 2024, has a seven-
day yield of 5.19%. 
 
The proposed budget is a decrease in the budget from the prior year. The proposed budget of 
$1,049,049 is $187,101 less than fiscal year 2024, brings in $500,000 from reserves, accounts for a 
$20,000 grant from the Cheyenne-Laramie Co. Economic Development Joint Powers Board, and $5,000 
in funding from Laramie County. The City contributes $209,929 covering all payroll, insurance, and 
benefits for employees. Other City staff also support DDA employees at no cost to the DDA. 
 
Highlights of the budget are below.   
 
• 
Visit Cheyenne sponsored events: 
o 
July, Downtown Express Shuttle, $7,000.00 
o 
July, Cheyenne Day, $5,000.00 
o 
September, OktoberFest/Comm App Day, $10,000.00 
o 
October, Downtown Halloween, $4,700.00 
o 
December, Old West Holiday, $15,000.00 
o 
December, Running of the Santas, $1,800.00 
o 
December, New Year's Eve, $5,000.00 
o 
April, Tales & Ales Pub Crawl, $1,500.00 
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  Planning and Development Department 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Suite 202, Cheyenne WY 82001 
(Phone) 307-637-6282 (Fax) 307-637-6366 

 
• 
DDA Board Strategic Projects 
This line item replaces the previous “Large Projects” line item and provides clarity that this is a 
special fund to carry our unique Board projects.  
 
• 
Sponsorship 
This line item represents sponsorships to other events and organizations that may make 
requests throughout the year. Allocations will be made on a request basis and sponsorship 
funds must face public facing activities and programs that have a direct public benefit.  
 
• 
Downtown Planter Program  
This program has been increased based on discussions with potential providers.   
 
• 
Façade Improvement Program (FIP) 
This is a preservation-based facade improvement program aimed at revitalizing historical 
structures by restoring and enhancing their exterior appearance while fostering economic 
growth through increased property values and tourism. The program has not been used in that 
capacity recently and staff intends to further develop the program in the upcoming year. For 
budgeting and accounting purposes, we recommend that this and the Capital Improvement 
Grant program be independent line items.     
 
• 
Capital Improvement Grant (CIG) Program  
This program has been commonly used in recent years and is comprised of a majority of the 
grants awarded in recent years. In fiscal year 2024, $500,000 was budgeted and it is expected 
that all grants will be allocated prior to the end of the fiscal year. For budgeting and accounting 
purposes, we recommend that this and the Façade Improvement Program be independent line 
items.     
 
• 
Sidewalk Match Program 
This is a new line item to carry out the “Sidewalk Repair Assistance Program” which will be in 
response to the City Engineering Department’s sidewalk repair initiative.  
 
• 
Logo Design Program 
Staff recommends zeroing out this program because logo and brand development results in a 
copyrighted product exclusive to a private party. Additionally, a similar program exists through 
the Wyoming Small Business Development Center Network which offers logo design assistance. 
 
The DDA Board may elect to adopt the budget as presented or make modifications. If the DDA Board 
desires more discussion on this item, this can be postponed to a special meeting next week. 
 
The DDA Board approved this budget at their April 18, 2024. 
 
Attachments: 
1. Resolution 

RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE BUDGET OF THE CHEYENNE 
DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY FOR THE FISCAL YEAR 
BEGINNING JULY 1, 2024 AND ENDING JUNE 30, 2025." 
 
WHEREAS, the Downtown Development Authority ("DDA") was created pursuant to 
Ordinance No. 2804 adopted by the Governing Body on July 9, 1984; 
 
WHEREAS, Wyoming Statute 15-9-208(a) requires the DDA to propose a budget, which 
shall be administratively reviewed by the City prior to submission of the proposed budget to the 
Governing Body for approval; 
 
WHEREAS, Cheyenne City Code, Chapter 2.76.050(C), provides that the DDA budget 
shall be subject to alteration, modification and approval by the Governing Body in accordance 
with the policies of the City; 
 
WHEREAS, the DDA Board approved the proposed budget at their April 18, 2024, 
meeting; and 
 
WHEREAS, City staff has reviewed the proposed budget received from the DDA .  
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the proposed budget for the Fiscal Year beginning July 1, 
2024 and ending June 30, 2025 of the Downtown Development Authority, as set forth in the 
attached information with this Resolution entitled "Downtown Development Authority Proposed 
Budget - Fiscal Year 2025" in the total amount of $1,049,049.00, is hereby approved. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 
6:32 pm, May 06 2024

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_29552073.pdf
RESOLUTION NO. __________ 
 
ENTITLED: "A RESOLUTION SUPPORTING THE ADOPTION OF A VISION ZERO 
POLICY TO ELIMINATE FATALITIES AND SERIOUS INJURIES THAT 
ARE A RESULT OF CRASHES ON CITY STREETS WITHIN THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE BY 2040." 
 
WHEREAS, the life and health of all persons living and traveling within the city of 
Cheyenne are the city's utmost priority, and no one should die or be seriously injured while 
traveling on city streets; 
 
WHEREAS, the Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization ("MPO") created a 
complete streets policy in 2006 as part of Plan Cheyenne, intending to improve safety and 
infrastructure for all road users; 
 
WHEREAS, the city of Cheyenne’s transportation infrastructure serves an increasing 
number of vulnerable road users such as pedestrians and bicyclists; 
 
WHEREAS, twenty-five (25) people in the Cheyenne urban area lost their lives to traffic 
deaths from 2019 through 2022, and traffic crashes are among the leading cause of deaths in the 
United States; 
 
WHEREAS, according to the Cheyenne MPO, in the period from 2019 through 2022, 
seventy-five (75) crashes involved pedestrians and forty (40) crashes involved bicyclists, resulting 
in eight (8) fatal crashes involving pedestrians and one (1) fatal crash involving a bicycle; 
 
WHEREAS, the number of people dying and suffering serious injuries on streets within 
the city of Cheyenne is a serious public health problem which necessitates public action; 
 
WHEREAS, Vision Zero is the concept that traffic deaths and serious injuries on our 
roadways are unacceptable and preventable; 
 
WHEREAS, Vision Zero sets the goal of eliminating traffic deaths and serious injuries in 
a set time frame with clear, measurable strategies;  
 
WHEREAS, Vision Zero recognizes that people will sometimes make mistakes, so the road 
system and related policies should be designed to ensure that those inevitable mistakes do not 
result in severe injuries or fatalities; therefore, transportation planners and engineers 
11:57 am, May 22 2024

and policymakers are expected to improve the roadway environment, policies, and other related 
systems to lessen the severity of crashes; 
 
WHEREAS, making streets safer for all people using all modes of transportation will 
encourage people to travel on foot, by bicycle, and by public transit, which supports a healthier, 
more active lifestyle and reduces environmental pollution;  
 
WHEREAS, Vision Zero aligns with the MPO's Complete Streets policy and complements 
the Wyoming Department of Transportation’s Safe Systems Approach to eliminate serious injuries 
and deaths;  
 
WHEREAS, successful Vision Zero programs are a result of both a complete government 
approach (i.e., interdepartmental, coordinated initiatives) and community support of Vision Zero 
objectives and action plans; and 
 
WHEREAS, Vision Zero resolutions have been adopted by many jurisdictions across the 
United States. 
 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED BY THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY 
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, that the City set a Vision Zero policy goal of eliminating traffic-
related fatalities and serious injuries in the city of Cheyenne by 2040. 
 
BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the city Engineer, city Planning and Development 
Department and the Metropolitan Planning Organization consider this goal in all future 
transportation planning and traffic safety decision. 
 
PRESENTED, READ AND ADOPTED this _______ day of _____________________, 
2024. 
 
 
__________________________________________  
Patrick Collins, Mayor  
 
 
(SEAL)  
 
 
ATTEST: 
 
____________________________________  
Kristina F. Jones, City Clerk 


--- END OF FILE ---

MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
May 7, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Landmark 
Aquatics, for the Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project 
 ______________________________________________ 
Three (3) Responsive Proposals were submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #8-24, for the 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project. 
  
The proposals were received from:  
1. Landmark Aquatics 
2. TDSI, Inc. 
3. Stockwell Engineers 
 
Upon review of the proposals by the evaluation panel, the group has recommended acceptance of 
the proposal submitted by Landmark Aquatics, for a project award amount of $236,957.00 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications and Cost based review and scoring of proposals. 
 
Funding:   
 032-17-1721-45-43113 (SPOT 2021 Ballot / Johnson Pool Project) 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 1 of 7 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
JOHNSON POOL EVALUATION AND DESIGN 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Landmark Aquatic, located at 505 Violet Street, Golden, Colorado 80401 
(“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature 
affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the Johnson Pool Evaluation 
and Design Project, in accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for Proposal (RFP) 
#8-24, incorporated herein by reference, and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, 
incorporated herein by reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by October 3, 2024. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant Two Hundred Fifteen 
Thousand Dollars ($215,000.00) for the base scope, Twenty-One Thousand, Nine 
Hundred Fifty-Seven Dollars ($21,957.00) for the alternate roof structure scope, for 
a total not to exceed Two Hundred Thirty-Six Thousand, Nine Hundred Fifty-Seven 
Dollars ($236,957.00), as stated in Exhibit A – Price Proposal. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
11:23 am, May 07 2024

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 2 of 7 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 3 of 7 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 4 of 7 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, the Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to October 3, 2024.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 5 of 7 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 6 of 7 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of 
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental 
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health 
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health 
agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar 
infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence 
beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or 
conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance 
required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without 
liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be 
refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing 
that he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of seven (7) pages, Exhibit A – Price Proposal, consisting of 
two (2) pages, Request for Proposal RFP 8-24, issued via OpenGov E-Procurement 
Portal, Addendum One, issued April 11, 2024 3:57 PM via OpenGov E-Procurement 
Portal, and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, consisting of thirty-seven (37) pages, 
contains the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, 
written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the Parties to this Agreement, through their duly authorized 
representatives, have executed this Agreement on the days and dates set out below and certify 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project

that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement.  This Agreement may be executed in multiple counterparts, each 
of which shall be deemed an original, but all of which together shall constitute one and the same 
instrument.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_72401081.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Erin Gates 
 
DATE: 
May 8, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
Consideration of RFP #9-24 for a Professional Service Agreement between the 
City of Cheyenne and TPT Trails, LLC design and construction services for the 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail project.  
 
 
Proposals were solicited for the Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build project. This project is for 
the design services and construction of approximately 6.5 miles of a single-track trail system for 
multi-use, nonmotorized use. One written technical proposal, with a sealed price proposal, was 
received from TPT Trails, LLC. 
 
A review committee comprised of City Staff from Public Works, Community Recreation and 
Events, Planning and Development, 1% Construction, and Engineering evaluated the submitted 
proposal for a qualification-based selection. The price proposal was opened following the ranking 
of the submitted proposal. The price proposal appears appropriate for the scope of services 
described in the Request for Proposals and the submitted written technical proposal. 
 
The fees include services design, construction, and GIS data for this project. Funding for this 
project will come from the Belvoir Recreation Fund, GL 015-21-2111-45-21301. The total 
professional and construction services will be a cost not-to-exceed $219,672.93. We respectfully 
request your approval of this professional services agreement.

EXHIBIT A 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail 
Scope of Work, Page 1 of 4 
 
Scope of Work 
 
Design 
The Consultant/Contractor shall design a trail system consisting of approximately 6.5 miles of new 
natural tread multi-use nonmotorized trails designed for Hikers and Mountain Bikers. The trail width 
will be 24 to 30 inches with a low maintenance sustainable tread design. The trail system shall be 
configured, developed, and constructed as a stacked loop system. Trail design and construction 
shall adhere to the IMBA Trail Solutions standards. Trails will be designed and built to accommodate 
users with an intermediate fitness level and beginner to intermediate mountain bike skills. This 
project does include a short segment of trail that will require advanced mountain bike skills. The 
project will also include signposts and navigational sign placement at all trail intersections. 
Directional/location signs will be supplied by the City of Cheyenne.   
 
Trail design must incorporate adequate grade reversals for trails to properly drain and shed water. 
Wherever possible, trails should be located and constructed on rock exposures and soils that 
support sustainability and reduce maintenance. Sustainability components should include: 
• 
Support of current and future use with minimal impact to the area’s natural systems. 
• 
Produce negligible soil erosion, loss, or movement while allowing vegetation to inhabit the 
area. 
• 
Does not adversely affect the area’s animal life. 
• 
Accommodate existing use while allowing only appropriate future use. 
• 
Careful trail design that eliminates or minimizes future trail rerouting.  
• 
Minimizes long-term maintenance. 
 
Construction 
Trail construction will be a combination of hand and machine-built tread. It should be anticipated 
that much of the trail alignment is not conducive to machine use due to rock outcroppings and other 
natural features that should remain undisturbed and minimally impacted for aesthetic value and 
preservation of the natural resource.  
 
Construction considerations include: 
• 
The tread of any trail section steeper than 8% and longer than 25 feet is to be armored with 
stone. The same requirement holds for any section longer than 10 feet where the grade 
exceeds 10%, and for the entire length of any section steeper than 12%.  
• 
Full bench cut construction technique wherever possible, otherwise partial bench cut with 
large rock edge stabilization. Drainage features should be incorporated into the edge 
stabilization.   
• 
Minimum finished tread width of 24 inches where side slopes are 40% or less. Where side 
slopes exceed 40%, or where downhill exposure exceeds 3 feet, the finished tread width of 
30 inches, minimum. 
• 
Average tread out-slope of 3% to 5%. 
• 
Trail tread surface will be a smooth finish except for unavoidable obstacles or trail 
stabilization features measuring 8 inches or less in height. Any rock ramp or step features 
incorporated into the trail tread should be shaped or built in a manner that does not create a 
trail feature that exceeds an intermediate difficulty rating.  
• 
Shaped back slope no steeper than 1:1 ratio except where impractical due to terrain 
features.  

EXHIBIT A 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail 
Scope of Work, Page 2 of 4 
 
• 
Fills deeper than 3 inches should incorporate a mix of dirt, crushed rock, and mineral soils. 
• 
Grade reversals incorporated as frequently as possible; ideally a minimum spacing of 100 
feet apart.  
• 
Climbing turns will have a minimum radius of 10 feet for intermediate trails and include a 
grade reversal before and after. 
• 
Directional/location trail maps will be placed at each intersection. 
• 
Trail maps will be oriented such that the top of the map is North and users viewing the map 
will be facing a Northern direction. 
 
LAYOUT: A preliminary trail corridor should be mapped and laid out with colored flagging at least 
every 50 feet with the desired route and with the IMBA Trail Difficulty Level matrix in mind. The actual 
trail should be placed within 20 feet of the corridor flag line, at no point will the average trail grade 
exceed 10% for more than 15 feet. At no point will the grade exceed 15%. The contractor shall field 
layout and pin flag the trail using curvilinear design principles to fit the specifications while staying 
within the trail corridor.  The City of Cheyenne project manager shall approve any major changes 
outside the area of the defined trail corridor prior to construction. Approval of the trail’s final field 
layout and flagging will be contingent on the Contractor meeting the specifications of the trail as laid 
out in this scope of work. City of Cheyenne staff will walk the trail centerline with the Contractor for 
final approval of field design prior to construction.   
 
PRESERVATION OF VEGETATION: The Consultant/Contractor shall exercise care to preserve the 
natural landscape, including trees and shrubs, and shall conduct construction operations to prevent 
any unnecessary destruction, scarring, or defacing of the natural surroundings in the vicinity of the 
work. Except where clearing is required for permanent work or excavation operations, all trees, 
native shrubbery, and vegetation, shall be preserved and protected from damage by the 
Consultant/Contractor's construction operations and equipment. All unnecessary destruction, 
scarring, damage, or defacing of the landscape resulting from the Consultant/Contractor's 
operations, shall be repaired, replanted, reseeded, or otherwise corrected as directed by the City’s 
Project Manager and at no additional expense to the owner.  
 
No living trees greater than 4” in diameter at 4 feet high will be cut or removed from the trail corridor.  
Tree stumps in the trail tread will be dug up and the hole filled with dirt. Trees and brush cut from the 
corridor shall be removed to a distance of at least 20 feet from the centerline on the downhill side of 
the trail. Cut trees and brush shall be cut up so they lay near the ground, not exceeding three feet in 
height. 
 
TRAIL CORRIDOR: Consultant/Contractor shall clear and grub the trail corridor and shape the trail 
tread to include the construction of backslopes, embankment and backfill, climbing turns, drainage 
structures, etc. leaving an even, compacted, uniform surface free of indentations. Native materials 
such as rocks, trees, soils, etc. within 100 feet of either side of the trail corridor centerline can be 
used to construct trail features and structures when it has been determined that their use will not 
adversely impact the natural resources of the area based on a field review between City’s Project 
Manager and the Consultant/Contractor during the field design and layout.  Native materials outside 
of 100 feet of the trail corridor centerline may be used with approval of the City’s Project Manager. 
 
TRAIL TREAD: Native mineral soil with a cross slope of 3 to 5% for sheet drainage. All organic 
materials shall be removed leaving an even, compacted, uniform surface free of indentations. 

EXHIBIT A 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail 
Scope of Work, Page 3 of 4 
 
Organic materials shall be scattered from the trail on the downhill side. Organic materials are not to 
be used for cut and fill. 
 
TRAIL GRADE/WIDTH: Trail width will be 24 to 30 inches.  The average trail grade should be 8% or 
less, and at no point will the average trail grade exceed 10% for more than 15 feet.  At no point will 
the grade exceed 15%. Trail grades shall not exceed 50% of the side-slope. For example, on a sidehill 
where the side-slope is 20%, the trail grade may not exceed 10%.  The trail width should be a 
minimum of 24 inches and maximum of 30 inches. 
 
TRAIL CROSS SECTIONS: Trail will be fully bench-cut into side-slope wherever possible. When 
bench-cut is not possible and material is needed to build up the downhill edge, it will be reinforced 
with a retaining wall made from natural on-site rock. Trail will be out-sloped 3 to 7% except corners 
where in-slope is needed for safety and usability. Back-slopes will be cut/shaped to maintain stable 
conditions but in general around a 1.5:1 slope ratio.  
 
TRAIL DIMENSIONS:   
• 
Tread Width  
 
24 to 30 inches 
 
• 
Corridor Width  
48 to 60 inches 
• 
Corridor Height 
 
84 to 120 inches 
• 
Out slope  
 
3% to 5% 
  
DRAINAGE: Drainage structures will be incorporated into the trail tread and trail layout to minimize 
the effects of water flow and prevent excessive erosion. Trail shall follow the contour of the terrain 
but remain at least 35 feet from stream banks or obvious surface water flow lines except at 
crossings. Alignment shall take advantage of natural drainage to minimize the need for major 
drainage modifications. In areas where there is a potential for trail erosion, grade reversals shall be 
incorporated into the trail tread to create natural appearing drainage dips. Grade reversals can 
include both grade dips and rolling grade dips. More grade reversals will be needed on steeper 
sections (over 7%) and less needed on flatter sections (under 4%). Water bars will not be used in the 
construction of the trail. 
 
ACCESS AND HAUL ROUTES: Visual damage to vegetation, either woody or herbaceous, must be 
avoided outside of the corridor. Use of and types of mechanical equipment will need to be approved 
by City’s Project Manager prior to construction. The Consultant/Contractor shall inspect the access 
for suitability and obtain permission from the City of Cheyenne for equipment transport to remote 
areas. The Consultant/Contractor shall make his own investigation of the condition of available 
public or private roads to determine clearances, restrictions, and other limitations that affect 
transportation at the job site. Existing roads are available for the Consultant/Contractor's use 
provided they are inside the boundary of the Belvoir Ranch property.  The Contractor will repair any 
damage on existing roads caused by the Contractor. 
 
POWER AND WATER:  The Consultant/Contractor shall make all necessary arrangements and shall 
provide all power and water required for construction purposes. The cost of conveying water to 
points of use shall be included in the bid price. 
 
SIGNAGE INSTALLATION: The Consultant/Contractor shall install signposts and navigational 
signage. Directional and location signs will be supplied by the City of Cheyenne. 
 

EXHIBIT A 
Belvoir Ranch Phase 2, Design-Build Trail 
Scope of Work, Page 4 of 4 
 
NON-MOTORIZED CATTLE GUARD: The Consultant/Contractor shall furnish and install a non-
motorized trail cattle guard, approximately 35” wide, with a handrail on one side (minimum), and 
pedestrian tread centered on the crossing, similar to those manufactured by Easy Fence, LLC, or 
approved equal. 
 
STAGING AREA:  The proposed main trailhead will be the staging area for the project.  Prior to 
construction the City’s Project Manager will identify and mark the location for on-site storage of 
equipment, materials, or other items needed for construction.  All tools and machinery should be 
cached within a reasonable distance from the work area. 
 
CLEAN-UP:  General clean-up will be carried out by the Consultant/Contractor over the limits of the 
entire project to the satisfaction of the Project Manager. This includes touch-up work, patching, and 
clean-up of all materials related to this contract and Consultant/Contractor. Clean-up will not be 
paid for separately but will be included in lump sum bid price. All waste materials will be packed out 
and sanitation practices shall adhere to “Leave No Trace” concepts. The Consultant/Contractor 
shall provide and use a portable toilet for human solid wastes. 
 
COORDINATION: The Consultant/Contractor should be aware of other construction activities that 
may be occurring in the same project area and shall coordinate with the city and others completing 
work at the site to minimize conflicts and meet project objective and deadlines. 
 
SCHEDULE: Construction shall be completed by November 30, 2024. 
 
DELIVERABLES 
Develop a plan for a stacked loop trail system that adheres to IMBA Trail Solutions standards. Trails 
should be designed and built for trail users with an intermediate fitness level and beginner to 
intermediate mountain bike skills. Identify main corridor routes with flagging approximately 50 feet 
apart.  Verify evaluation of rock and soil conditions to determine appropriate features and grades for 
maximum trail sustainability.   
 
Prepare a draft plan that includes:   
• 
Detailed maps identifying main corridor routes  
• 
Scenic overlooks 
• 
Rock outcroppings 
• 
Climbing turns 
• 
Switchbacks / berm corners 
• 
Rock and wood features 
• 
Culverts or sustainable drainage crossings 
• 
Retaining walls 
The plan shall include details that will be used during construction. A description of means and 
methods of trail and feature construction shall be provided. Prepare a cost estimate for construction 
based on the map and identified features. 
 
Build approximately 6.5 miles of non-motorized, natural tread trail to IMBA Trail Solutions standards. 
Provide final GIS data of the constructed trails.

  Award Executive Summary:  This grant applies for Section 5307 formula funds totaling

The only project in this application will be to purchase 10 replacement vehicles for the 
transit program at a cost-sharing ratio of 85/15, within the next three years. 
Local funds will be provided by local general funds and non-federal state funds. 
Pre-award authority is requested starting 1/1/2023 for $850,000 for the purchase of 6 
replacement vehicles. 
Required documentation is attached to the application, including the Governor's 
apportionment letter, and TIP/STIP documentation. 
The Safety and Security requirements will not be met with funding from this application, 
as those requirements are being covered by the City's Police and Fire Departments with 
funding outside of this application.

activities.

  Suballocation Funds:  Recipient organization is directly allocated these funds and is 
eligible to apply for and receive these funds directly. 
  Pre-Award Authority:  This award is using Pre-Award Authority.

PREAMBLE 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into this _____ day of _____________, 2024, 
by and between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne Metropolitan Planning Organization, hereinafter 
referred to as the “MPO”, and Quandel Consultants, Inc., hereinafter referred to as “Consultant.”  
The City of Cheyenne is acting as the agent of the MPO for the contract administration and as the 
MPO’s Fiscal Manager in accordance with Contract #______. 
AGREEMENT 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
1.
SCOPE OF WORK. The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-
mentioned project, in accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A,
incorporated by reference and expressly made a part hereof.
2.
TIME OF PERFORMANCE. The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed
in accordance with the project schedule attached hereto as Exhibit B, incorporated by
reference and expressly made a part hereof.  Any extensions of the time limit set forth in
the Scope of Work shall be agreed upon in writing between the parties.
3.
COMPENSATION. In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this
Agreement, the Consultant will bill the MPO and the MPO agrees to pay Consultant a total
cost-not-to-exceed amount of one hundred ninety-seven thousand, nine hundred forty-four
dollars and ninety cents ($197,944.90), in accordance with the fee schedule provided in
Exhibit C, which is attached to this Agreement, incorporated by reference, and expressly
made a part hereof. The amounts of all such payments shall be based upon the Consultant’s
progress, verified by the MPO, in completing the work as described in Exhibits A, B, and
C. Final payment shall be made following acceptance of the work by the MPO. Original
quality reproductions of all designs, plans, reports, specifications, drawings, and other
services rendered by the Consultant shall become the sole property of the MPO and shall
be delivered to the MPO, immediately upon their preparation to the extent the MPO has
satisfied its payment obligations to the Consultant. A portion of the payment may be held
until any claims are resolved.
4.
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY. The Consultant shall be responsible for the
professional quality, technical accuracy, timely completion of drawings and other services
6:30 pm, May 20 2024

Professional Services Agreement 
for Passenger Rail Station Site Selection Study 
Page 2 of 19 
rendered by the Consultant and its sub-consultants and shall, without additional 
compensation, promptly remedy and correct any errors, omissions, or other deficiencies, 
except to the extent that such errors, omissions or other deficiencies arise from the digital 
data created by Cheyenne/Laramie County Cooperative Geographic Information System 
(CLCCGIS). 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the Consultant 
and by mutual agreement between the parties, the Consultant will, without additional 
compensation, correct those services not meeting such a standard. 
5.
GIS MAPS AND DIGITAL DATA. If the Consultant uses any maps, coverages, images,
or other digital data created by the CLCCGIS for the project specified in Exhibit A, the
Consultant agrees to return or destroy that information once the project is complete.
Consultant agrees not to reuse or sell the GIS maps or data, which were provided as a
professional courtesy and to minimize the cost of the project.
6.
GIS DATA LIMITATION AND DISCLAIMED LIABILITY. GIS data is collected
primarily for use by the City of Cheyenne and Laramie County. Any unauthorized use of
the data is at the risk of the user.
7.
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS. The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations
as provided in this Agreement in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances.
8.
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE. In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of
November 1988, the City of Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled
Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and drug usage by City employees. All architects,
engineers, and other consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees
and sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or sub-
consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated.
9.
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS. The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold
harmless and defend the City from and against any and all liabilities, claims, penalties,
forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses incident thereto, including reasonable
attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result of death or bodily injury to any person,
destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations or orders to the extent
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2)

any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or sub-
consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges that it

10.
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS. The Consultant must provide proof of the following
insurance coverages: 
Commercial General Liability Insurance. For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness 
or death, or from damage to or destruction of property of others, including loss or use 
thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the 
entire term of the Agreement. 
Business Automobile Insurance. Including owned, non-owned and hired vehicles with 
minimum limits for bodily injury and property damage of $1,000,000 per accident for the 
entire term of the Agreement. 
Workers’ Compensation. Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire 
term of the Agreement, as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing 
services under this Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ 
compensation or employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
Professional Liability Insurance. The Consultant shall provide proof of professional 
liability insurance or errors and omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than 
$500,000 to protect the City from any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s 
negligence in the performance of duties under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this 
liability insurance coverage be provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
a.
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, provide the
City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be
in effect at the time the Consultant begins the provision of services under this
Agreement;
b.
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a new
certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be in effect
during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services under this
Agreement;
c.
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five (5) years
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this Agreement;
and
d.
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” coverage prior
to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., b., or c. of this

paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance written notification of

provided services under this Agreement. 
Additional Insurance Information. The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne and 
the MPO as an Additional Insured by endorsement on its insurance policies, with the

REMEDIES. In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall

a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not exceed the 
contract amount; or 
 
b. 
Treat the agreement as continuing and require specific performance.

TERMINATION BY MPO. The MPO Representative may, without cause, by written

a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
b. 
Deliver to the MPO Representative within five (5) days all data, drawings, 
specifications, reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and 
materials as may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the MPO will pay the Consultant for 
accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. Such 
acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld.

January 29, 2024 
Cheyenne Station Area Plan 
Scope of Work  
STUDY OBJECTIVES 
•
Conduct an analysis of three locations for the Cheyenne passenger rail station as identified by
the Client.  This analysis can assist in your efforts to consolidate support for the recommended
location and can provide key material in your efforts for future funding of the station.  A more
complex FRA Alternative Analysis study will be needed, as part of future phases of the project,
that will incorporate this study’s material to expedite completion of the process.
•
Complete railroad operational issue for the addition of both Amtrak’s Pioneer and Front Range Passenger 
Rail (FRPR) services to existing freight service on BNSF’s Front Range Subdivision (FRPR) and UP’s
Greeley Subdivision (Amtrak Pioneer) as it relates to trackage improvements near and adjacent to
potential station sites.
•
Identify facility amenities and other Amtrak and FRPR requirements related to passenger station
design.
•
Evaluate station area opportunities and issues
STUDY TASKS 
The proposed study would be composed of the tasks identified below and would be directed by the Client 
Project Manager. Work on the study tasks will be performed by the Consultant staff, with assistance and 
cooperation from City of Cheyenne, the Cheyenne MPO, and Laramie County. The following sections 
provide specific detailed information on the work associated with each of this project’s tasks. 
TASK 1: Project Management 
The Consultant will provide ongoing project management under this over-arching task and will coordinate 
closely with the Mayor’s Passenger Rail Commission, MPO and City staff. Key staff from the Consultant, 
City and MPO will be members of the Project Study Team. Meetings of this Team will be held bi-weekly, 
generally via conference call. Meeting agendas will be prepared prior to the meetings by the Consultant, 
in conjunction with the Client Project Manager. Meeting notes will be distributed to Project team 
members within three working days after each meeting.  One on-site meeting is planned. 
The Consultant will develop a Project Management Plan (PMP) to provide the approach used in tracking 
project schedule and budget. The PMP will include the Project Scope, Deliverables, and Schedule and the 
approach to manage quality and document control for the Project. 
Project administration and invoicing are also included in Task 1. 
Task 1 Deliverables: 
EXHIBIT A-Scope of Work

2 
Project Management Plan 
Project Schedule 
Attendance at bi-weekly meetings (8) 
Summary notes from meetings  
TASK 2: Station Location Alternatives Evaluation 
The Project Study Team will evaluate three potential station sites within the study area. Two sites include 
the existing Union Pacific Railroad (UP) depot location (or vicinity) and the Reed Avenue Corridor (or 
vicinity).  To identify the additional site beyond the identified two within the Cheyenne area, criteria for 
site selection will be established during a site selection kickoff meeting.  The criteria will be used to score 
the location alternatives.  The Project Study team will analyze the identified sites meeting most, or all, of 
the established criteria and create an initial site matrix identifying potential additional sites.  
The three sites will be vetted using the selection criteria at a ½-day workshop with the MPO and other 
identified stakeholders. This workshop will provide a shortlist of no more than two sites that will be 
evaluated in more detail as proposed below. The two finalist sites that will be vetted further in Task 5 for 
selection of a single preferred alternative. 
In making recommendations as to a preferred site for the future rail station, the Project Study Team will 
evaluate the size of site, potential transit interface, passenger rail rider accessibility, street access, parking, 
fit within the community and neighborhood, planned uses for the adjacent areas, development, 
redevelopment, and adaptive reuse opportunities, pedestrian and bicycle access; impacts on railroad 
operations, proximity to other transportation modes and other relevant circumstances. The potential 
station sites will be evaluated in detail through a second matrix process and will be ranked by the Project 
Team with input from representatives/officials of the community. The deliverable from this task will 
include the selection of two finalist site alternatives which find the “sweet spot” between providing 
passenger convenience and minimizing train delays due to backing or other train operations issues 
experienced in Cheyenne’s previous passenger rail history. 
Explore the possibility of utilizing just one station to accommodate both Amtrak and FRPR service.  If two 
different locations are required for these services, describe the issues related to the area being served by 
different stations.  

3 
Task 2 Deliverables: 
Site Selection Kickoff Meeting 
MPO Site Selection Workshop 
Site Selection Matrix  
Sketch of potential sites/facilities 
Technical memoranda describing the alternatives analysis methodology and presenting the results of the 
analysis  
TASK 3: Railroad Operations Analysis 
For the selected two alternatives, the Project Team will meet with Amtrak, BNSF, and the UP to identify any 
effects (positive or negative) which the proposed Amtrak and Front Range service will have on existing or 
projected passenger and freight rail service operating in the study area.  The Consultant will prepare a 
description of the existing track alignment issues as discussed in these meetings.  
Task 3 Deliverables: 
Coordination meetings with Amtrak (3) 
Coordination meetings with BNSF (2) 
Coordination meetings with UP (2) 
Technical memorandum – A description of Railroad Meetings, Results and Future Recommendations 
TASK 4: Railroad Infrastructure Modifications / Improvements 
The Consultant will develop conceptual railroad infrastructure improvement plans for the two finalist station 
locations to accommodate the possible reinstatement of Pioneer Amtrak service proposed for Cheyenne as 
well as future FRPR service. Station platform requirements for Amtrak and Federal Railroad Administration 
(FRA) will be reviewed for the potential sites.  Since FRPR trains will likely be shorter in length than Amtrak’s 
long-distance trains, the design specifications for Amtrak should suffice for FRPR trains. The Railroad 
Infrastructure Improvements includes the following elements: 
•
Rail Infrastructure Improvement Concepts
•
Station Platform Concepts
Task 4 Deliverable: 
Description of Amtrak and FRA requirements relevant to a Cheyenne Passenger Rail Station 

4 
TASK 5: Environmental and Economic Development Impacts 
For the two finalist station locations identified in Task 2, the Project Team will perform a high-level, 
desktop analysis of environmental and community impacts of the project, including: wetlands, 
contamination issues, impacted community facilities, noise and vibration impacts, traffic impacts, multi-
modal linkages, private property acquisitions (if any), and other relevant factors identified. With respect to 
community impacts, the Consultant will conduct up to eight virtual meetings with the MPO to review the 
proposed station locations for conformance with Land Use, Community Plans and Zoning. 
A technical memorandum will be developed to summarize economic development opportunities for each 
of the two sites that will illustrate the potential for increased revenue to the City due to increased 
property values and private sector investment and development opportunities attributed to passenger rail 
facilities. Included as a work element of the economic development impact task is an evaluation of options 
pertaining to station ownership and examining community development patterns to identify strategies 
which will encourage private sector development and investment. 
The environmental and economic development considerations and findings for the two contending sites will 
be documented and through this process, a single preferred alternative site will be recommended. The 
recommended site will exhibit all or most of the identified criteria in Task 2 as well as show the most 
favorable impact on economic development for the City and region and the most minimal impact on the 
environment. 
Task 5 Deliverables: 
•
Technical memorandum documenting environmental desk top analysis
•
Technical memorandum documenting potential for economic development
•
Technical Memorandum:  Minutes of virtual meetings with the MPO
TASK 6: Station Facility Components 
With consideration of outcomes ascertained from the above tasks and public input, the Project Team will 
prepare recommendations on the components and facilities to be included in conceptual station designs. 
We will identify components to meet both Front Range and Amtrak requirements summarized below: 
-
Waiting Facility with restroom facilities with consideration for how to control access to limit vandalism
-
Platform – height and design standards, canopy
-
Driveway Access – access to pick-up/drop-off areas
-
Parking areas
-
ADA requirements Transit and taxi stop and on-site circulation
-
Station signage
-
Station lighting
-
Way finding signage
-
Supportive uses (Retail, commercial (lessor possibly responsible for cleaning and security)

5 
-
Electronic ticketing and kiosks Security measures
-
Bicycle racks
-
Fencing
-
LEED Certification
-
Facilities for snow management including platform plows (or electric heaters), garage for equipment, and
salt storage
-
Other facilities and design elements
Included as part of this task is the incorporation of projected ridership data from Amtrak for the proposed 
service. This data will also inform the size requirements for the actual station facility based on projected 
usage by passenger rail customers utilizing the through-train service. These projections and forecasts will be 
used to support activities in the Station Facility Design and quantifying space needs of design elements. 
Task 6 Deliverables: 
•
Technical memorandum describing the design elements noted above
•
Preliminary sketch plans of up to three station options (including the recommended alternative)
TASK 7: Preparation of Draft Document and Presentation of Preliminary Recommendations 
Based upon input obtained and the information ascertained from the above tasks, the Draft Cheyenne 
Station Area Study will be prepared. This document can be posted by the MPO for public comment.  
Included as part of this task are briefings with the MPO TAC on the results of the study. 
Task 7 Deliverables: 
•
Draft Cheyenne Station Area Study report
•
Graphics and Conceptual sketches
•
Presentation of Study Results to MPO
TASK 8: Preparation of Final Document 
This last task consists of finalizing the study report, the recommendations that identify the key elements to 
be included in the preferred passenger rail station design, and rail infrastructure improvements. 
Recommendations will also be included outlining the next steps by which to further this initiative and to re-
establish passenger rail service, including options related to funding of capital improvements. 
Task 8 Deliverables: 
•
Final Cheyenne Station Area Study report
•
Graphics and Conceptual sketches
