# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_16625155.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
May 21, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and The Design 
Studio, Inc. (TDSi), for the Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project 
 ______________________________________________ 
Two (2) Responsive Proposals were submitted under Request for Proposal (RFP) #8-24, for the 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project. 
  
The proposals were received from:  
1. TDSI, Inc. 
2. Stockwell Engineers 
 
The Proposal submitted by Landmark Aquatics has been rejected due to non-conformance with 
the scope requirements. 
 
Upon review of the proposals by the evaluation panel, the group has recommended acceptance of 
the proposal submitted by The Design Studio, Inc. (TDSi), for a project award amount of 
$247,533.00. 
At this time, the Scope of Work requests Sixty-Five Percent (65%) Design Development (DD), 
but does not include completion of One-Hundred Percent (100%) Construction Documents (CD).  
This may be considered at a future time.   
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications and Cost based review and scoring of proposals. 
 
Funding:   
 032-17-1721-45-43113 (SPOT 2021 Ballot / Johnson Pool Project) 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 1 of 9 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
JOHNSON POOL EVALUATION AND DESIGN 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and The Design Studio, Inc. (TDSi), located at 1610 Pioneer Avenue, Cheyenne, 
Wyoming 82001 (“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the 
last signature affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the Johnson Pool Evaluation 
and Design Project, for Sixty-Five Percent (65%) Design Development (DD), in 
accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for Proposal (RFP) #8-24, incorporated 
herein by reference, and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, incorporated herein by 
reference. 
 
1.1. 
CONSTRUCTION DOCUMENTS PHASE SERVICES 
1.1.1 Construction Documents Phase Services for this project are not included 
in this agreement. 
1.1.2. The Owner may request, in writing, that the Architect provide 
Construction Documents Phase Services after the date of this agreement. If the 
Architect agrees in writing to provide such services, the Architect shall be 
compensated as noted in Article 3 below. 
1.1.3. If the Owner elects to not retain the Architect for Construction Documents 
Phase Services: 
1.1.3.1.ºThe Owner shall be solely responsible for interpreting the 
Schematic Design and Design Development Phase Documents to discover, 
correct and mitigate any errors, inconsistencies, or omissions, and for 
authorizing deviations from the documents during the development of the 
Contract Documents. 
1.1.3.2 The Owner shall indemnify and hold the Architect, its agents and 
employees harmless from and against all claims, losses, damages and 
expenses, including third party claims, losses, damages and expenses, and 
10:29 am, May 21 2024

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 2 of 9 
including but not limited to the defense costs and the time of the Architect, 
regardless of whether or not such claim, loss, damage or expense is caused 
in part by a party indemnified under this agreement related to the Owner’s 
or a Third Parties’ interpretation of the Schematic Design and Design 
Development Phase Documents, or the Owner’s or Third Parties’ failure 
to discover, correct or mitigate any errors, inconsistencies or omissions 
from the Schematic Design and Design Development Phase Documents, 
and for authorizing or making any deviations to the design from the 
Schematic Design and Design Development Phase Documents. 
 
1.2. 
BIDDING, 
CONSTRUCTION 
AND 
POST 
CONSTRUCTION 
PHASE 
SERVICES 
 
1.2.1 Bidding, Construction and Post Construction Phase Services for this 
project are not included in this agreement. 
1.2.2. The Owner may request, in writing, that the Architect provide Bidding, 
Construction and Post Construction Phase Services after the date of this 
agreement. If the Architect agrees in writing to provide such services, the 
Architect shall be compensated for such services as noted for Additional 
Services. 
1.2.3. If the Owner elects to not retain the Architect for Bidding, Construction 
and Post Construction Phase Services: 
1.2.3.1.ºThe Owner shall be solely responsible for interpreting the 
Contract Documents and observing the Work of the Contractor to 
discover, correct and mitigate any errors, inconsistencies, or 
omissions, and for authorizing deviations from the construction 
documents. 
1.2.3.2 The Owner shall indemnify and hold the Architect, its agents and 
employees harmless from and against all claims, losses, damages 
and expenses, including third party claims, losses, damages and 
expenses, and including but not limited to the defense costs and the 
time of the Architect, regardless of whether or not such claim, loss, 
damage or expense is caused in part by a party indemnified under 
this agreement related to the Owner’s interpretation of the Contract 
Documents, observation of or failure to adequately observe the 
Work of the Contractor, and discovery, and directions to correct or 
mitigate any errors, inconsistencies or omissions, or the Owner’s 
failure to discover, correct or mitigate any errors, inconsistencies 
or omissions from the construction document, and for authorizing 
any deviations from the construction documents. 
 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 3 of 9 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by October 3, 2024.  The 
Consultant shall perform its services as expeditiously as is consistent with such 
professional skill and care and the orderly progress of the Project. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant Two Hundred 
Forty-Seven Thousand, Five Hundred Thirty-Three Dollars ($247,533.00), as stated 
in Exhibit A – Price Proposal.   
 
In the event that the City modifies the Agreement to include the remaining Scope of 
Work items to bring the Project to One Hundred (100%) Percent Construction 
Documents (CD) as documented in Exhibit A, the requested amount to be added to this 
Agreement may be revised or increased.  The amount of revision or increase shall not 
exceed the percentage of current inflation as identified in the Consumer Price Index 
(CPI).  This increase shall be calculated from the Effective Date of this Agreement to the 
date the revised Agreement Modification is prepared. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 4 of 9 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
To the fullest extent permitted by law, Consultant shall indemnify the City of Cheyenne, 
its officers, directors, partners, employees, and representatives, from and against losses, 
damages, and judgments arising from claims by third parties, including reasonable 
attorneys’ fees and expenses recoverable under applicable law, but only to the extent they 
are found to be caused by a negligent act, error, or omission of Consultant or 
Consultant’s officers, directors, members, partners, agents, employees, or subconsultants 
in the performance of services under this Agreement. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy. 
 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 5 of 9 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, the Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 6 of 9 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to March 31, 2026.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 7 of 9 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 8-24 
Johnson Pool Evaluation and Design Project  
Page 8 of 9 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of 
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental 
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health 
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health 
agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar 
infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence 
beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or 
conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance 
required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without 
liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be 
refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing 
that he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of nine (9) pages, Exhibit A – Price Proposal, consisting of 
fourteen (14) pages, Request for Proposal RFP 8-24, issued via OpenGov E-Procurement 
Portal, Addendum One, issued April 11, 2024 3:57 PM via OpenGov E-Procurement 
Portal, and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, consisting of thirty-four (34) pages, 
contains the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, 
written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter.

of which shall be deemed an original, but all of which together shall constitute one and the same

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_12820956.pdf
MEMORANDUM 
TO: 
 
Mayor Patrick Collins, and City Council 
FROM: 
 
TJ Barttelbort, Purchasing Manager 
DATE: 
 
May 21, 2024 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Services Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Just Be You, 
Inc. dba Star Playgrounds, for the Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation 
Project 
 ______________________________________________ 
Three (3) Proposers responded to Request for Proposal (RFP) #11-24, for the Saddle Ridge 
Playground Design and Installation Project. 
  
The proposals were received from:  
1. Just Be You, Inc. dba Star Playgrounds  
2. Great Western Installations, Inc  
3. Dakota Playground 
 
The Request for Proposal dictated that proposers were given the opportunity to submit proposals 
for a $200,000 price point, and a $300,000 price point for playground design proposals.  The three 
(3) Proposers submitted a total of eight (8) unique playground designs that were evaluated 
independently. 
Upon review of the proposals by the evaluation panel, the group has recommended acceptance of 
the proposal submitted by Just Be You, Inc. dba Star Playgrounds (Playground Design Option – 
C, Rendering Included), for a project award amount of $300,000.00. 
 
Basis for award was based on a Qualifications and Cost based review and scoring of proposals. 
 
Playground installation to be completed by November 29, 2024. 
 
Funding:   
013-21-2111-45-43702  
(Development Impact Fees / Saddle Ridge Park Enhancements)

 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 11-24 
Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation  
Page 1 of 7 
City Contract No. ____________ 
 
PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
SADDLE RIDGE PLAYGROUND DESIGN AND INSTALLATION 
 
This Professional Services Agreement (“Agreement”) is made and entered into by and 
between the City of Cheyenne, located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 
(“City”), and Just Be You, Inc. dba Star Playgrounds, located at 9892 Titan Park Circle, 
Unit 1, Littleton, Colorado 80125 (“Consultant”).  This Agreement shall become effective 
upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the Saddle Ridge Playground 
Design and Installation, in accordance with City of Cheyenne Request for Proposal 
(RFP) #11-24, incorporated herein by reference, and the Consultant’s submitted 
proposal, incorporated herein by reference. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by November 29, 2024. 
 
3. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the 
Consultant will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant Three Hundred 
Thousand Dollars ($300,000.00), as stated in Exhibit A – Price Proposal. 
 
4. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the 
Consultant, the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services 
not meeting such a standard. 
 
5. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement 
in accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
6. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug-Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of 
Cheyenne has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to 
11:15 am, May 20 2024

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 11-24 
Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation  
Page 2 of 7 
alcohol and drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers, and other 
consultants under contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and 
sub-consultants, are required to comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and 
Controlled Substance Policy for drug and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites 
occupied by the Consultant while performing the duties and responsibilities of the 
Agreement. It is the responsibility of the Consultant to familiarize themselves with the 
requirements of this policy and to inform all their employees and sub-consultants of the 
requirements and ensure their compliance. If the Consultant, their employees, or 
sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the Agreement may be terminated. 
 
7. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless, and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures, and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a 
result the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any 
person, destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations, or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error, or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or 
sub-consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges 
that it may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
8. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness, or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 
per occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be 
provided pursuant to an “occurrence” policy. 
  
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 11-24 
Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation  
Page 3 of 7 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, 
the Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City 
with a new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or 
shall be in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide 
services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs 
a., b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City 
advance written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall 
provide the City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period 
(“tail coverage”) which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than 
five (5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services 
under this Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the 
above-stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All 
policies required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the 
Agreement.  It shall be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify 
in writing the city risk manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or 
occurrence that has resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change 
to any insurance coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be 
construed to be a breach of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall 
not affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 11-24 
Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation  
Page 4 of 7 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
9. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin, or sex. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, the Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a 
written statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, 
and work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing 
of any payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities 
that may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing 
in this Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or 
employees to act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any 
obligation of any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, 
debts, and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this 
Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
The term of this Agreement shall be from date of execution to November 29, 2024.  The 
parties may by mutual written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 11-24 
Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation  
Page 5 of 7 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. 
Upon receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the 
Consultant for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of 
termination. Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, 
the City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of 
default of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default 
to commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear 
its own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation, and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary 
for the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring 
experts and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in 
progress; to examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this 
Agreement; and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 11-24 
Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation  
Page 6 of 7 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne 
and shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request. 
 
21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
FORCE MAJEURE. 
The performance of the Agreement by either party shall be subject to force majeure 
including, but not limited to, acts of God, fire, flood, natural disaster, war or threat of 
war, acts or threats of terrorism, civil disorder, unauthorized strikes, governmental 
regulation or advisory, recognized health threats as determined by the World Health 
Organization, the Centers for Disease Control, or local government authority or health 
agencies (including, but not limited to, the health threats of COVID-19, H1N1, or similar 
infectious diseases), curtailment of transportation facilities, or other similar occurrence 
beyond the control of the parties, where any of those factors, circumstances, situations, or 
conditions or similar ones prevent, dissuade, or unreasonably delay the performance 
required by this Agreement. The Agreement may be cancelled by either party, without 
liability, damages, fees, or penalty, and any unused deposits or amounts paid shall be 
refunded, for any one or more of the above reasons, by written notice to the other party. 
 
23. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing 
that he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
24. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of seven (7) pages, Exhibit A – Price Proposal, consisting of 
two (2) pages, Request for Proposal RFP 11-24, issued via OpenGov E-Procurement 
Portal, Addendum One, issued May 1, 2024 3:26 PM via OpenGov E-Procurement 
Portal, Addendum Two, issued May 8, 2024 7:52 AM via OpenGov E-Procurement 
Portal and the Consultant’s submitted proposal, consisting of twenty-eight (28) pages, 
contains the entire understanding of the parties. There are no other terms or conditions, 
written or oral, concerning or controlling this matter. 
 

 
City of Cheyenne / Professional Services Agreement / RFP 11-24 
Saddle Ridge Playground Design and Installation

that they have read, understood, and knowingly and voluntarily agreed to the terms and 
conditions of this Agreement.  This Agreement may be executed in multiple counterparts, each 
of which shall be deemed an original, but all of which together shall constitute one and the same 
instrument.

# SOURCE_FILE: CHEY-2024-05-08_TotalExtraction_38637200.pdf
 
 
 
 
 
MEMO 
 
TO: 
 
Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne 
 
FROM: 
Thomas D. Cobb, PE 
 
DATE:  
May 22, 2024 
 
SUBJECT: 
Professional Service Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and WHS, for 
Cheyenne – Three Alleyway Project Design Services.  
 
 
Please find the attached Professional Service Agreement between the City and WHS for 
engineering services related to the design and construction of the Cheyenne – Three Alleys 
Project.  
  
Purpose of Project: 
The primary purpose of the project is to begin the rehabilitation of the alleys within the DDA 
boundary per the Specific Purpose Option Tax (6th Penny) ballot.  Rehabilitation in this case is defined 
as drainage, surfacing maintenance, and reconstruction improvements as required to increase 
serviceability and decrease future mainteance costs.      
 
The 2021 6th Penny Ballot stated in “PROPOSITION #10: $6,017,966 TO CITY OF CHEYENNE AND 
THE INTEREST EARNED THEREON FOR DOWNTOWN IMPROVEMENTS AND MAINTENANCE 
PROJECT TO IMPROVE AND MAINTAIN INFRASTRUCTURE SUCH AS ROADS, PARKING, CURBS, 
GUTTERS, SIDEWALKS, LIGHTING AND ALLEYS IN THE DDA DISTRICT; TO OPERATE AND 
MAINTAIN THE DEPOT PLAZA.” 
 
The initial three alleys selected included the following: 
 
▪ 
Alley 1 – 300’ x 12’ St. Mary’s Alley from Capitol Ave. to Central Ave. 
▪ 
Alley 2 - 300’ x 12’ Alley north of 18th Street from Warren Ave. to House Ave. 
▪ 
Alley 3 - 300’ x 12’ Alley north of 16th Street from Central Ave. to Capitol Ave. 
 
Formal discussions have taken place with Saint Mary’s Cathedral, and they have agreed to proceed 
with an agreement to split costs for both the design and construction of Alley No. 1 under a 
separate agreement.   It is anticipated that this alley will then be vacated and given to the church for 
all future maintenance and rehabilitation.  
 
 
 
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
  
OFFICE OF THE CITY ENGINEER 
2101 O’Neil Avenue, Room 206  
Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 

Honorable Mayor Patrick J. Collins, City of Cheyenne 
Members of the City Council, City of Cheyenne

Selection Process 
WHS was selected by the Engineering Department utilizing the Statement of Qualifications on file 
with our office and based on a quality-based selection process which follows the Purchasing Policy 
Guide of the City of Cheyenne. 
 
Cost: 
The fee includes professional engineering services for development of final construction 
documents including project management, surveying, permitting, bidding services, and 
stakeholder coordination.  
 
The total professional services will be a cost not-to-exceed of $110,246. Therefore, we 
respectfully request the use of the Specific Purpose Option Tax (6th Penny) ballot 
Proposition No. 10, for use on this design project.   The balances as of April 30, 2024 were as 
follows:  Ballot Amount:  $6017,966; Collections Received:  $1,228,246; Encumbrances; 
($469,726); Balance Less Encumbrances:   $2,941,955.

PROFESSIONAL SERVICES AGREEMENT 
for 
Cheyenne - Three Alleyway Project Design Services  
 
THIS AGREEMENT is made and entered into by and between the City of Cheyenne, 
located at 2101 O’Neil Avenue, Cheyenne, Wyoming 82001 (“City”), and William H. Smith and 
Associates (WHS), located at 1515 9th St, Ste A, Rock Springs, Wyoming 82901 (“Consultant”).  
This Agreement shall become effective upon the date of the last signature affixed hereto. 
 
RECITALS 
 
In consideration of the mutual covenants and obligations herein expressed, it is agreed by 
and between the parties as follows: 
 
1. 
SCOPE OF WORK 
The Consultant agrees to provide services as described for the above-mentioned project, in 
accordance with the Scope of Work attached hereto as Exhibit A, incorporated by reference 
and expressly made a part hereof. 
 
2. 
TIME OF PERFORMANCE 
The work included in this Scope of Work will be completed by January 1, 2025. 
 
3. 
CONSULTANT RESPONSIBILITY 
In providing services under this Agreement, the Consultant shall perform in a manner 
consistent with that degree of care and skill ordinarily exercised by members of the same 
profession currently practicing under similar circumstances. Upon notice to the Consultant, 
the Consultant will, without additional compensation, correct those services not meeting 
such a standard. 
 
4. 
COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS 
The Parties agree that they will perform their obligations as provided in this Agreement in 
accordance with all applicable laws and ordinances. 
 
5. 
DRUG-FREE WORKPLACE 
In compliance with the Drug Free Workplace Act of November 1988, the City of Cheyenne 
has established an Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy that pertains to alcohol and 
drug usage by City Employees. All architects, engineers and other consultants under 
contract with City of Cheyenne, and their employees and sub-consultants, are required to 
comply with the provisions of the City’s Alcohol and Controlled Substance Policy for drug 
and/or alcohol usage on City property or other sites occupied by the Consultant while 
performing the duties and responsibilities of the Agreement. It is the responsibility of the 
Consultant to familiarize themselves with the requirements of this policy and to inform all 
their employees and sub-consultants of the requirements and ensure their compliance. If 
9:02 am, May 24 2024

Cheyenne - Three Alleyway Project Design Services  
Page 2 of 9 
the Consultant, their employees or sub-consultants are found in violation of this policy, the 
Agreement may be terminated. 
 
6. 
INDEMNIFICATION/HOLD HARMLESS 
The Consultant agrees to indemnify, hold harmless and defend the City from and against 
any and all liabilities, claims, penalties, forfeitures and suits, and the cost and expenses 
incident thereto, including reasonable attorney’s fees, which may hereafter arise as a result 
the performance of the Consultant’s duties, including death or bodily injury to any person, 
destruction or damage to any property, contamination of or adverse effects on the 
environment, or any violation of governmental laws, regulations or orders to the extent 
caused by (1) the Consultant’s breach of any term or provision of this Agreement; or (2) 
any negligent or wrongful act, error or omission by the Consultant, or its employees or sub-
consultants in the performance of this Agreement.  The Consultant acknowledges that it 
may incur a financial obligation to the City pursuant to the terms of this paragraph. 
 
7. 
INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS 
The Consultant must provide proof of the following insurance coverages: 
 
 
Commercial General Liability Insurance 
For claims arising out of bodily injury, illness or death, or from damage to or destruction 
of property of others, including loss or use thereof, with minimum limits of $1,000,000 per 
occurrence, $2,000,000 aggregate for the entire term of the Agreement. 
 
 
Workers’ Compensation 
Workers’ compensation coverage shall be in effect for the entire term of the Agreement, 
as required by Wyoming law, for all employees or agents providing services under this 
Agreement.  Consultant shall provide the City with proof of workers’ compensation or 
employer’s liability insurance coverage. 
 
 
Professional Liability Insurance 
The Consultant shall provide proof of professional liability insurance or errors and 
omissions liability insurance in an amount not less than $500,000 to protect the City from 
any and all claims arising from the Consultant’s negligence in the performance of duties 
under this Agreement.  The City prefers that this liability insurance coverage be provided 
pursuant to an “occurrence” policy.  
 
 
If this coverage is provided pursuant to a “claims made” policy: 
 
 
 
a. 
Consultant shall, concurrently with the execution of this Agreement, 
provide the City with a certificate of insurance demonstrating that such 
coverage is or shall be in effect at the time the Consultant begins the 
provision of services under this Agreement; and 
 
 
b. 
In the event the Consultant’s services extend into a future policy period, the 
Consultant shall, prior to the policy expiration date, provide the City with a 
new certificate of insurance demonstrating that such coverage is or shall be 

Cheyenne - Three Alleyway Project Design Services  
Page 3 of 9 
in effect during all periods of time that Consultant will provide services 
under this Agreement; and 
 
 
c. 
Consultant shall maintain said “claims made” coverage for a period of five 
(5) years following the last date that Consultant has provided services under 
this Agreement; and 
 
 
d. 
In the event the Consultant or the insurer terminates “claims made” 
coverage prior to the expiration of the periods provided in subparagraphs a., 
b., or c. of this paragraph, the Consultant shall provide to the City advance 
written notification of the termination of said coverage and shall provide the 
City with an endorsement for an extended reporting period (“tail coverage”) 
which shall be in effect for a period of time not less than five (5) years 
following the last date that Consultant has provided services under this 
Agreement.  
 
 
Additional Insurance Information 
 
The Consultant shall name the City of Cheyenne as an Additional Insured by 
endorsement on its insurance policies and shall provide the City with a copy of the 
endorsements.  This requirement does not apply to workers’ compensation and 
professional liability insurance policies. 
 
Consultant shall provide the City with certificates of insurance acknowledging the above-
stated coverages prior to beginning any work under this Agreement. 
 
It is understood and agreed that these policies are primary and not contributory. All policies 
required under this Agreement shall be in effect for the duration of the Agreement.  It shall 
be an affirmative obligation upon Consultant to immediately notify in writing the city risk 
manager, city clerk, and city attorney of any fact, circumstance, or occurrence that has 
resulted in or may result in the cancellation of, or substantive change to any insurance 
coverage required by this Agreement, and failure to do so shall be construed to be a breach 
of this Agreement. 
 
If requested by the City, the Consultant shall provide the City with copies of insurance 
policies and/or policy endorsements listing the City of Cheyenne as an additional insured.  
The City’s failure to request or review such policies, endorsements, or certificates shall not 
affect the City’s rights or Consultant’s obligation hereunder. 
 
Any insurance company providing coverage under this Agreement shall have a minimum 
A. M. Best rating of A- (excellent). 
 
8. 
MINORITY AND DISADVANTAGED BUSINESS ENTERPRISES 49 C.F.R. Part 26 
All parties to this Agreement assure that no person will be excluded from participation in, 
denied the benefits of, or otherwise discriminated against, in connection with the award 
and performance of this Agreement on the grounds of age, race, color, disability, national 
origin or sex.

9. 
COMPENSATION 
In consideration of the services to be performed pursuant to this Agreement, the Consultant 
will bill the City and the City agrees to pay Consultant a not to exceed amount of One 
Hundred Ten Thousand Two Hundred Forty-Six. ($110,246) stated in Exhibit B. 
 
10. 
MONTHLY REPORT 
With every monthly billing, Consultant shall provide the City Representative with a written 
statement of the status of the work with respect to the Scope of Work, time sheets, and 
work schedule. Failure to provide the required monthly report will delay processing of any 
payment request until the report is submitted. 
 
11. 
INDEPENDENT CONSULTANT 
The Consultant shall function as an independent consultant for the purposes of this 
Agreement. The Consultant shall assume sole responsibility for any debts or liabilities that 
may be incurred by the Consultant in fulfilling the terms of this Agreement. Nothing in this 
Agreement shall be interpreted as authorizing the Consultant or its agents or employees to 
act as an agent or representative of or on behalf of the City or to incur any obligation of 
any kind on the behalf of the City. 
 
12. 
TAXES 
The Consultant agrees to pay all valid taxes, excises, license fees, permit fees, bills, debts 
and obligations incurred by and in connection with its operations under this Agreement. 
 
13. 
DEFAULT 
Each and every term and condition in this Agreement shall be deemed to be a material 
element of the Agreement. In the event either party should fail or refuse to perform 
according to the terms of this Agreement, such party may be declared in default. 
 
14. 
REMEDIES 
In the event a party has been declared in default, such defaulting party shall be allowed a 
period of fifteen (15) days within which to cure the default.  In the event the default 
remains uncorrected, the non-defaulting party declaring default may elect to: 
 
 
a. 
Terminate the Agreement and seek damages, which damages shall not 
exceed the contract amount; or 
 
 
b. 
Treat the Agreement as continuing and require specific performance. 
 
15. 
TERM AND TERMINATION 
This Agreement shall end on or before January 1, 2025.  The parties may by mutual 
written agreement renew or extend this Agreement. 
The City may, without cause, and upon thirty days’ written notice to the Consultant, 
terminate this Agreement in whole or in part at any time, for the City’s convenience. Upon 
receipt of such notice, the Consultant shall: 
 
 
a. 
Discontinue all services affected; and 
 
 
b. 
Deliver to the City within five (5) days all data, drawings, specifications, 
reports, estimates, summaries, and such other information and materials as 

Cheyenne - Three Alleyway Project Design Services  
Page 5 of 9 
may have been accumulated by the Consultant in performing this 
Agreement, whether completed or in process. 
 
 
c. 
In the event of termination for convenience, the City will pay the Consultant 
for accepted work done and expenses incurred to the date of termination. 
Such acceptance shall not be unreasonably withheld. 
 
16. 
ADDITIONAL REMEDIES 
In the event the Consultant fails to strictly perform in accordance with this Agreement, the 
City may elect to correct the deficiencies and charge the Consultant. In the event of default 
of any of the conditions by either party which shall require the party not in default to 
commence legal or equitable action against the defaulting party each party shall bear its 
own costs and expenses, including without limitation, attorney’s fees.  
 
17. 
GOVERNING LAW, JURISDICTION AND VENUE 
The construction, interpretation and enforcement of this Agreement shall be governed by 
the laws of the State of Wyoming.  The courts of the State of Wyoming shall have 
jurisdiction over any action arising out of this Agreement and over the parties, and the 
venue shall be the First Judicial District, Laramie County, Wyoming. 
 
18. 
GOVERNMENTAL IMMUNITY 
The City of Cheyenne, along with its officials and employees, does not waive its 
governmental immunity by entering into this Agreement, except to the extent necessary for 
the parties to pursue a contract action to clarify or enforce the written terms of the 
Agreement.  Furthermore, the City specifically retains all immunities and defenses 
available to it as a sovereign or governmental entity pursuant to Wyo. Stat. § 1-39-101, et 
seq., and all other relevant state and federal law. Designations of venue, choice of law, 
enforcement actions, and similar provisions should not be constructed as a waiver of 
governmental immunity. The parties agree that any ambiguity in this Agreement shall not 
be strictly construed, either against or for either party, except that any ambiguity as to 
governmental or sovereign immunity shall be construed in favor of governmental 
immunity. 
 
19. 
MONITORING ACTIVITIES 
The City shall have the right to monitor all activities related to this Agreement that are 
performed by the Consultant or its sub-consultants. This shall include, but not be limited 
to, the right to make site inspections at any time and with reasonable notice; to bring experts 
and consultants on site to examine or evaluate completed work or work in progress; to 
examine the books, ledgers, documents, papers, and records pertinent to this Agreement; 
and to observe personnel in every phase of performance of the related work. 
 
20. 
OWNERSHIP OF DOCUMENTS AND WORK PRODUCT 
All deliverables by the Consultant, all work product of the Consultant, all notes, 
calculations, memoranda, or any other documentation generated by the Consultant 
pursuant to the scope of this Agreement, shall be the property of the City of Cheyenne and 
shall be provided to the City of Cheyenne immediately upon request.

21. 
NON-DISCRIMINATION 
The Consultant shall comply with the Civil Rights Act of 1964, the Wyoming Fair 
Employment Practices Act (Wyo. Stat. § 27-9-105, et seq.), the Americans With 
Disabilities Act (ADA), as amended, 42 U.S.C. § 12101, et seq., and the Age 
Discrimination Act of 1975 and any properly promulgated rules and regulations thereto, 
and shall not discriminate against any individual on the grounds of age, sex, creed, color, 
race, religion, national origin, ancestry, pregnancy or qualifying disability in connection 
with the performance under this Agreement. 
 
22. 
PROFESSIONAL REGISTRATION 
The Consultant shall endorse, as required by law, plans and reports prepared under this 
Agreement, and shall affix thereto his or her seal of professional registration, showing that 
he or she is licensed to practice in the State of Wyoming, if necessary. 
 
23. 
ENTIRETY OF AGREEMENT 
This Agreement consisting of nine (9) pages, and the proposal, which contains of Exhibit 
A and Exhibit B for a total of Five (5) pages, contain the entire understanding of the parties. 
There are no other terms or conditions, written or oral, concerning or controlling this 
matter.

SUPPLEMENTAL CONDITIONS TO THE GENERAL CONDITIONS 
 
A. GENERAL CONDITIONS 
 
Standard of Care.  The standard of care for all professional engineering and related 
services performed or furnished by Engineer under this Agreement will be the care and 
skill ordinarily used by members of the subject profession practicing under similar 
circumstances at the same time and in the same locality.  The Engineer makes no 
warranties, express or implied, under this Agreement or otherwise, in connection with 
any services performed or furnished by Engineer.  
 
Technical Accuracy.  Owner shall not be responsible for discovering deficiencies in the 
technical accuracy of Engineer’s services.  Engineer shall correct deficiencies in 
technical accuracy without additional compensation unless such corrective action is 
directly attributable to deficiencies in Owner-furnished information.   
Conflicts within the Agreement.  If there is a conflict within this Agreement, the order 
of priority of the documents which make up this Agreement, from highest to lowest, will 
be: 
(a) Agreement. 
(c) Any supplementary conditions to the General Conditions. 
(d) General Conditions. 
(e) Exhibit A – SCOPE OF WORK. 
(f) Other schedules to this Agreement. 
 
Any changes in the Consultant’s or Subconsultant’s staff or fee structure will be presented 
in writing to the City for approval prior to initiating any changes or performing any work. 
 
Overhead and Normal Business Expenses.  The consultant shall include customary 
overhead costs and statutory benefits as part including, but not limited to, social security 
contributions, unemployment, excise and payroll taxes, workers’ compensation, health 
and retirement benefits, bonuses, sick leave, vacation, and holiday pay, as applicable, 
within the billing rates furnished in Exhibit B.  Additionally, the cost of general and 
administrative overhead which includes salaries and wages of employees engaged in 
business operations not directly chargeable to projects, plus non-Project operating costs, 
including but not limited to, business taxes, legal, rent, utilities, office supplies, 
insurance, and other operating costs shall not be directly billed to project and assumed to 
be included within the billing rates furnished. 
 
 
B. DESIGN PROCESS DOCUMENTATION 
 
To document the design process, the consultant will be required to provide a Decision

EXHIBIT A-PROJECT SCOPE
ALLEY DESIGN SCOPES
Alley 1 – 300’ x 12’ St. Mary’s Alley from Capitol 
Ave. to Central Ave.
Alley 2 - 300’ x 12’ Alley north of 18th Street 
from Warren Ave. to House Ave.
Alley 3 - 300’ x 12’ alley north of 16th Street 
from Central Ave. to Capitol Ave.
WHS WORK ITEMS:
•	
GIS/Cheyenne maps review – accuracy and 
utility capacity within 1 block radius 
•	
Utility locating by Nunn Locating 
•	
Shallow Geotechnical Investigation by JB 
Engineering, up to 4’ depth (bore holes and 
extensive sampling excluded due to access/
overhead obstructions) 
•	
Utility & Surface features surveys (2) – (up 
to 100’ corridor) 
•	
Kick-off meeting with the City of Cheyenne 
(1) 
•	
Stakeholder/adjacent business meetings  (1 
each + 1 collective review conference) 
•	
Preliminary Design Phase, Data Collection, 
Conceptual Design 
•	
50% Review Meeting with City of Cheyenne 
(1) 
•	
Final Design Phase, 11x17 construction Plan 
set creation (up to 10 sheets), permitting 
with local agencies, cost estimating (1) 
•	
Final Review Meeting with City of Cheyenne 
(1) 
•	
Project 
bid 
phase, 
pre-bid 
meeting, 
advertising, 
bidding, 
bid 
review 
and 
recommendations. (1 round)
CURRENT CONDITION
After touring the St. Mary’s site with Doug Klahn 
from the City of Cheyenne and the subsequent 
meeting on September 18th, 2023 with St. 
Mary’s Father Tom Cronkleton, Neill O’donnell, 
Director of Operations at St. Mary’s and Tom 
Cobb, City Engineer, we understand that the
issues in this alley are largely water related. 
Ice continually forms on the north side of the 
rectory due to slow surface drainage from the 
largely flat parking lot. The water pools on 
uneven concrete paving. This poor drainage is 
exacerbated by the additional water from the 
roof drains of the Rectory. This area remains 
in the shade from Fall to Spring making a large 
sheet of ice for patrons to try to cross to enter 
the building. WHS intends, with continual 
communication and discussions with the above 
stakeholders, to address the drainage by first
reviewing all available information on utilities 
and prior construction activity. WHS will survey 
the alley and adjacent parking lot to define 
the surface drainage paths. WHS will analyze 
the projected water volumes from both roof 
drainage and surface sheet flow. WHS intends 
to use Nunn locating to define all utility conflicts 
and subsurface obstructions using their ground 
penetrating radar which will result in a design 
that may include the  installation of storm 
sewer, inlets or slotted drains.
UTILITY, TRENCH, SUB-BASE
 AND BASE DESIGN
WHS will perform a geotechnical investigation 
to establish foundation support for the
pavement and any backfill for additional 
conveyance (piping) for the storm sewer, inlets 
and catch basins.
PAVEMENT DESIGN
WHS will design the pavement per city standards 
either in asphalt or concrete. Special attention 
will be given to ensure the pavement is properly 
graded, delivering the best drainage of the alley 
way and parking lot.
ROLL-OFF SANITATION CONTAINER 
ENCLOSURE
WHS will utilize the existing design document 
supplied by Mr. O’Donnell for the 3-sided
enclosure. WHS will validate the foundation 
design. WHS will investigate if the enclosure 
walls can be adequately supported on top of 
a designed reinforced-concrete slab. WHS will 
contact the masonry suppliers to acquire an 
appropriate matching brick, or brick fascia 
product.

PROPOSAL: CHEYENNE-THREE ALLEYS |
CURRENT CONDITIONS 
FOR ALLEY 2 AND 3
The city inspector Doug Klahn and Mark 
Madsen (WHS) toured the alley that runs 
behind Wallick and Volk, running East to West 
between Warren Avenue and House Avenue 
and the alley to the north of the Plains Hotel, 
running East to West from Central Avenue 
to Capitol Avenue. In both cases we found 
alleys with considerable use by surrounding 
businesses with access to the businesses and 
multiple vehicle parking spots. Both Alleys 
had deteriorated pavements and inadequate 
drainage.
